\vartheta:\mathbb{R}^{2}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
with compact support in a ball of radius
\approx\lambda_{{q+{\bar{n}}}}^{-1}
such that the following holds. Let
\varrho^{k}
and
\vartheta^{k}
be defined as in Proposition
4.4, []
, in terms of
\varrho
and
\vartheta
(instead of
\varkappa
). Then there exists
\mathbb{U}_{\pm\vec{e}_{2},\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}},r_{q}}^{k}:\mathbb{T}^{3}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{3}
such that
if
\{\vec{e}_{2},\xi^{\prime},\xi^{\prime\prime}\}\subset\mathbb{Q}^{3}\cap\mathbb{S}^{2}
form an orthonormal basis of
\mathbb{R}^{3}
with
\vec{e}_{2}\times\xi^{\prime}=\xi^{\prime\prime}
, then we have

\mathbb{U}_{\pm\vec{e}_{2},\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}},r_{q}}^{k}=-\frac{1}{3}\xi^{\prime}\underbrace{\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{-{\mathsf{D}}}\xi^{\prime\prime}\cdot\nabla\left(\mathrm{div}^{{\mathsf{D}}-2}\left(\vartheta^{k}\right)\right)^{ii}}_{=:\varphi^{\prime\prime k}}+\frac{1}{3}\xi^{\prime\prime}\underbrace{\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{-{\mathsf{D}}}\xi^{\prime}\cdot\nabla\left(\mathrm{div}^{{\mathsf{D}}-2}\left(\vartheta^{k}\right)\right)^{ii}}_{=:\varphi^{\prime k}}\,,

(3.10)

and thus

\mathrm{curl\,}\mathbb{U}_{\pm\vec{e}_{2},\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}},r_{q}}^{k}=\xi\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{-{\mathsf{D}}}\mathrm{div}^{\mathsf{D}}\left(\vartheta^{k}\right)=\vec{e}_{2}\varrho^{k}\,,

(3.11)

and

\vec{e}_{2}\cdot\nabla\vartheta^{k}=(\vec{e}_{2}\cdot\nabla)\mathbb{U}_{\pm\vec{e}_{2},\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}},r_{q}}^{k}=0\,.

(3.12)

(2)

The sets
\{\mathbb{U}_{\pm\vec{e}_{2},\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}},r_{q}}^{k}\}_{k}
,
\{\varrho^{k}\}_{k}
, and
\{\vartheta^{k}\}_{k}
satisfy the conclusions from
[
29
, Proposition 4.4]
.

(3)

For all
k\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
,

\displaystyle\|\Psi_{q}^{\pm}\|_{\infty}\lesssim 1\,,\qquad\|\nabla\Psi_{q}^{\pm}-\mathrm{Id}\|_{\infty}\lesssim\frac{\lambda_{q*}}{\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}}\,,\qquad\|\nabla^{1+k}\Psi_{q}^{\pm}\|_{\infty}\lesssim\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{-1}\lambda_{q*}^{1+k}\,.

(3.13)

(4)

For all
n\leq 3\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}
,

{\|\nabla^{n}\vartheta^{k}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{3})}\lesssim\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{n}r_{q}^{\left(\nicefrac{{2}}{{p}}-1\right)}},\qquad{\|\nabla^{n}\varrho^{k}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{3})}\lesssim\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{n}r_{q}^{\left(\nicefrac{{2}}{{p}}-1\right)}}

(3.14)

and abbreviating
\mathcal{H}^{k}_{\pm\vec{e}_{2},\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}},r_{q}}
by
\mathcal{H}^{k}
, we have the bounds
{\|\nabla^{n}\mathbb{U}_{\pm\vec{e}_{2},\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}},r_{q}}^{k}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{3})}\lesssim\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{n-1}r_{q}^{\left(\nicefrac{{2}}{{p}}-1\right)}}
and
{\left\|\nabla^{n}\mathbb{H}^{k}\right\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{3})}\lesssim\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{n}r_{q}^{\left(\nicefrac{{2}}{{p}}-1\right)}}
.

(5)

We have that
\mathrm{supp\,}\vartheta^{k}\subseteq B\left(\mathrm{supp\,}\varrho,2\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{-1}\right)
.

(6)

Let
\Phi:\mathbb{T}^{3}\times[0,T]\rightarrow\mathbb{T}^{3}
be the periodic solution to the transport equation

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\Phi+v\cdot\nabla\Phi=0\,,\qquad\Phi|_{t=t_{0}}

\displaystyle=x\,,

(3.15)

with a smooth, divergence-free, periodic velocity field
v
. Then

\nabla\Phi^{-1}\cdot(\mathbb{H}^{k}\circ\Phi)=\mathrm{curl\,}(\nabla(\Psi_{q}^{\pm}\circ\Phi)^{T}\cdot(\mathbb{U}_{\pm\vec{e}_{2},\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}},r_{q}}^{k}\circ\Psi_{q}^{\pm}\circ\Phi)).

(3.16)

(7)

The helical pipe bundles have the property that for each fixed
I
,

\displaystyle{\pm}(\boldsymbol{\rho}_{\vec{e}_{2}}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\vec{e}_{2}}^{I})^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}}{\lambda_{q*}^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}}\right)^{2}(\boldsymbol{\rho}_{\vec{e}_{2}}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\vec{e}_{2}}^{I})^{2}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.98003pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.26338pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.6363pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.45924pt}}\!\int_{\left(\frac{\mathbb{T}}{\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}r_{q}}\right)^{3}}\mathbb{H}^{k}_{{\pm}{\vec{e}_{2}},\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}},r_{q}}\cdot\mathrm{curl\,}\mathbb{H}^{k}_{{\pm}{\vec{e}_{2}},\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}},r_{q}}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}}{\lambda_{q*}^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}}\right)^{2}(\boldsymbol{\rho}_{\vec{e}_{2}}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\vec{e}_{2}}^{I})^{2}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.98003pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.26338pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.6363pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.45924pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathbb{H}^{k}_{{\pm}\vec{e}_{2},\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}},r_{q}}\cdot\mathrm{curl\,}\mathbb{H}^{k}_{{\pm}\vec{e}_{2},\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}},r_{q}}\,.

(3.17)

(8)

There exists a
q
-independent geometric constant
C_{\varrho}>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathrm{supp\,}\mathbb{H}^{k}_{{\vec{e}_{2}},\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}},r_{q}}(\cdot)\subset B(\mathrm{supp\,}\varrho^{k}_{{\vec{e}_{2}},\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}},r_{q}}(\cdot),C_{\varrho}\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{-1})\,.

(3.18)

Proof.

Since
\lambda_{q*}
and
\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}
are powers of two,
\Psi^{\pm}_{q}
is a diffeomorphism of
\mathbb{T}^{3}
.
After computing

\displaystyle\nabla\Psi_{q}^{\pm}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}1&\mp\frac{\lambda_{q*}}{\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}}\cos(\lambda_{q*}x_{2})&0\\
0&1&0\\
0&-\frac{\lambda_{q*}}{\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}}\sin(\lambda_{q*}x_{2})&1\end{pmatrix}\,,\quad(\nabla\Psi_{q}^{\pm})^{-1}=\begin{pmatrix}1&\pm\frac{\lambda_{q*}}{\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}}\cos(\lambda_{q*}x_{2})&0\\
0&1&0\\
0&\frac{\lambda_{q*}}{\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}}\sin(\lambda_{q*}x_{2})&1\end{pmatrix}\,,

item
3
follows immediately. Note importantly that
\Psi_{q}^{\pm}
is a volume-preserving diffeomorphism, since the determinant of
\nabla\Psi_{q}^{\pm}
is one. Then the proofs of item
1
,
2
,
4
,
5
, and
6
are identical to the corresponding proofs in Proposition
4.5, []
, and we omit the details.

We focus now on the proof of item
7
. By (
3.8
),

\displaystyle\mathrm{curl\,}\mathbb{H}_{\pm}

\displaystyle=\left(\nabla(\varrho(\Psi_{q}^{\pm}))\times\widetilde{e}_{2}^{\pm}+\varrho(\Psi_{q}^{\pm})\,\mathrm{curl\,}\widetilde{e}_{2}^{\pm}\right)\,,

(3.19)

\displaystyle\widetilde{e}_{2}^{\pm}

\displaystyle=\left(\pm\frac{\lambda_{q*}}{\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}}\cos(\lambda_{q*}x_{2}),1,\frac{\lambda_{q*}}{\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}}\sin(\lambda_{q*}x_{2})\right)^{\top}\,,

(3.20)

\displaystyle\mathrm{curl\,}\widetilde{e}_{2}^{\pm}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\lambda_{q*}^{2}}{\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}}\cos(\lambda_{q*}x_{2}),0,\pm\frac{\lambda_{q*}^{2}}{\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}}\sin(\lambda_{q*}x_{2})\right)^{\top}\,,

(3.21)

and so we apply the Pythagorean theorem and deduce that
\mathrm{curl\,}\widetilde{e}_{2}^{\pm}\cdot\widetilde{e}_{2}^{\pm}=\pm\frac{\lambda_{q*}^{3}}{\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{2}}
. Thus

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}}{\lambda_{q*}^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}}\right)^{2}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathbb{H}_{\pm}\cdot\mathrm{curl\,}\mathbb{H}_{\pm}\,dx=\left(\frac{\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}}{\lambda_{q*}^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}}\right)^{2}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho(\Psi^{\pm})^{2}\mathrm{curl\,}\widetilde{e}_{2}^{\pm}\cdot\widetilde{e}_{2}^{\pm}\,dx=\pm\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho(\Psi_{q}^{\pm})^{2}\,dx=\pm 1\,.

Finally, we must prove item
8
. Since
\|\Psi_{q}^{\pm}-\mathrm{Id}\|_{\infty}\leq\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{-1}
, the support of
\rho(\Psi_{{\bar{n}}})
is contained in a
\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{-1}
ball around the support of
\rho
, which gives the result.
∎

4
Mollification and upgrading material derivatives

The details in this section are largely similar to
[
29
, Section 3]
, and so we omit them.

Lemma 4.1

(
Mollification and upgrading material derivative estimates).

Assume that
all
inductive assumptions listed in subsections
2.2
-
2.7
hold. Let
\mathcal{P}_{q,x,t}
be a space-time mollifier with kernel a product of
\mathcal{P}_{q,x}(x)
, which is compactly supported in space at scale
\Lambda_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q-1}^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}
, and
\mathcal{P}_{q,t}(t)
, which is compactly supported in time at scale
\mathrm{T}_{q-1}\Gamma_{q-1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}
; we assume that both kernels have vanishing moments up to
10\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}
and are
C^{10\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}
-differentiable. Define the trace-free, symmetric
2
-tensor

\displaystyle R_{\ell}:=\mathcal{P}_{q,x,t}R_{q}^{q}-\frac{1}{3}\mathrm{tr\,}\left(\mathcal{P}_{q,x,t}R_{q}^{q}\right)\mathrm{Id}

(4.1)

on the space-time domain
[-\nicefrac{{\tau_{q-1}}}{{2}},T+\nicefrac{{\tau_{q-1}}}{{2}}]\times\mathbb{T}^{3}
.
Then the following hold.

(i)

The following relaxed equation (replacing (
2.8
)
) is satisfied:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}u_{q}+\mathrm{div}(u_{q}\otimes u_{q})+\nabla\left(p_{q}-\frac{1}{3}\mathrm{tr\,}R_{q}^{q}\right)=\mathrm{div}\left(R_{\ell}+\left(R_{q}^{q}-\frac{1}{3}(\mathrm{tr\,}R_{q}^{q})\mathrm{Id}-R_{\ell}\right)+\sum\limits_{k=q+1}^{q+{\bar{n}}-1}R_{q}^{k}\right)\,.

(ii)

The inductive assumptions in subsection
2.3
,
2.5
,
2.6
, and
2.7
remain unchanged.

(iii)

The inductive bounds for
R_{q}^{q}
in (
2.17
) are replaced by the following: for all
N+M\leq\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{i,q}D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}R_{\ell}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q}^{2}\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}}\left(\Lambda_{q}\Gamma_{q}\right)^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q}^{i}\mu_{q}^{-1},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\right)\,,

(4.2a)

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{i,q}D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}R_{\ell}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q}^{2+\mathsf{C_{\infty}}}\left(\Lambda_{q}\Gamma_{q}\right)^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q}^{i}\mu_{q}^{-1},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\right)\,.

(4.2b)

(iv)

The symmetric tensor
R_{\ell}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{3}}\mathrm{tr\,}R_{q}^{q}\mathrm{Id}-R_{q}^{q}
satisfies the following: for all
N+M\leq 2\mathsf{N}_{\rm ind}
,

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\left(R_{\ell}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{3}}(\mathrm{tr\,}R_{q}^{q})\mathrm{Id}-R_{q}^{q}\right)\right\|_{\infty}\lesssim\Gamma_{q+1}\mathrm{T}_{q+1}^{4\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}}}\delta_{q+3{\bar{n}}}^{2}\lambda_{q+1}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\mu_{q}^{-1},\Gamma_{q}^{-1}\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\right)\,.

5
New velocity increment

5.1 Preliminaries

We recall basic facts about cutoff functions and flow maps.

5.1.1 Time cutoffs

We introduce two collections of basic temporal cutoffs similar to
[
29
, subsection 5.1]
; the only differences are that sixth powers have been substituted for second powers, and that
\mu_{q}
has been substituted for
\tau_{q}
. The first set of temporal cutoff functions
\chi_{i,k,q}(t)
satisfies

\displaystyle t\in\mathrm{supp\,}\chi_{i,k,q}\iff t

\displaystyle\in\left[(k-1)\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\mu_{q}\Gamma_{q}^{-i-2},(k+1)\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\mu_{q}\Gamma_{q}^{-i-2}\right]\,,

(5.1a)

\displaystyle|\partial_{t}^{m}\chi_{i,k,q}|

\displaystyle\lesssim(\Gamma_{q}^{i+2}\mu_{q}^{-1})^{m}\qquad\textnormal{for }m\geq 0\,,

(5.1b)

\displaystyle\chi_{i,k_{1},q}(t)\chi_{i,k_{2},q}(t)

\displaystyle\equiv 0\qquad\qquad\textnormal{unless }|k_{1}-k_{2}|\leq 1\,

(5.1c)

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\chi_{i,k,q}^{2}

\displaystyle\equiv 1\,.

(5.1d)

The second set of temporal cutoff functions
\widetilde{\chi}_{i,k,q}
is only needed for technical reasons (see Lemma
5.11
) and is supported in the set of times
t
satisfying

\left|t-\mu_{q}\Gamma_{q}^{-i-2}k\right|\leq\mu_{q}\Gamma_{q}^{-i}\,.

(5.2)

In addition, if
(i,k)
and
({i^{*}},{k^{*}})
are such that
\mathrm{supp\,}\chi_{i,k,q}\cap\mathrm{supp\,}\chi_{{i^{*}},{k^{*}},q}\neq\emptyset
and
{i^{*}}\in\{i-1,i,i+1\}
, then with a sufficiently large choice of
\lambda_{0}
, we have that
\mathrm{supp\,}\chi_{i,k,q}\subset\mathrm{supp\,}\widetilde{\chi}_{{i^{*}},{k^{*}},q}
.

5.1.2 Velocity cutoffs

Recall from subsection
2.6
that velocity cutoffs have already been constructed up to
{q+{\bar{n}}}-1
. The new velocity cutoffs
\psi_{i,{q+{\bar{n}}}}
are constructed in Definition
9.5
.

5.1.3 Flow maps

We first recall two results from
[
29
, Section 5.2]
. The only difference is cosmetic– the timescale
\tau_{q}
has been replaced by
\mu_{q}
.

Lemma 5.1

(
Lagrangian paths don’t jump many supports).

Let
q^{\prime}\leq q+{\bar{n}}-1
and
(x_{0},t_{0})
be given. Assume that the index
i
is such that
\psi_{i,q^{\prime}}^{2}(x_{0},t_{0})\geq\kappa^{2}
, where
\kappa\in\left[\frac{1}{16},1\right]
. Then the forward flow
(X(t),t):=(X(x_{0},t_{0};t),t)
of the velocity field
\widehat{u}_{q^{\prime}}
originating at
(x_{0},t_{0})
has the property that
\psi_{i,q^{\prime}}^{2}(X(t),t)\geq\nicefrac{{\kappa^{2}}}{{2}}
for all
t
such that
|t-t_{0}|\leq\mu_{q^{\prime}}\Gamma_{q^{\prime}}^{-i+4}
.

Corollary 5.2

(
Backwards Lagrangian paths don’t jump many supports).

Suppose
(x_{0},t_{0})
is such that
\psi^{2}_{i,q^{\prime}}(x_{0},t_{0})\geq\kappa^{2}
, where
\kappa\in\left[\nicefrac{{1}}{{16}},1\right]
. For
\left|t-t_{0}\right|\leq\mu_{q^{\prime}}\Gamma_{q^{\prime}}^{-i+3}
, define
x
to satisfy
x_{0}=X(x,t;t_{0})
. Then we have that
\psi_{i,q^{\prime}}(x,t)\neq 0
.

We can now define and estimate the flow of the vector field
\widehat{u}_{q^{\prime}}
for
q^{\prime}\leq q+{\bar{n}}-1
on the support of a velocity and time cutoff function. These estimates are completely analogous to
[
29
, subsection 5.2]
. We do note, however, that we have sharpened the estimates in (
5.4a
)–(
5.4b
), which is important for our estimations of the helicity of the velocity increment.

Definition 5.3

(
Flow maps).

We define
\Phi_{i,k,q^{\prime}}(x,t)=\Phi_{(i,k)}(x,t)
as the solution to

(\partial_{t}+\widehat{u}_{q^{\prime}}\cdot\nabla)\Phi_{i,k,q^{\prime}}=0\,\qquad\qquad\Phi_{i,k,q^{\prime}}(x,k{\mu_{q^{\prime}}}\Gamma_{q^{\prime}}^{-i-2})=x\,.

(5.3)

We denote the inverse of
D\Phi_{(i,k)}
by
(D\Phi_{(i,k)})^{-1}
, in contrast to
D\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1}
, the gradient of
\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1}
.

Corollary 5.4

(
Deformation bounds).

For
k\in\mathbb{Z}
,
0\leq i\leq i_{\rm max}
,
q^{\prime}\leq q+{\bar{n}}-1
, and
2\leq N\leq\nicefrac{{3\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{2}}+1
, we have the following bounds on the set
\Omega_{i,q^{\prime},k}:=\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,q^{\prime}}(x,t){\widetilde{\chi}_{i,k,q^{\prime}}(t)}
:

\displaystyle\left\|D\Phi_{(i,k)}-{\rm Id}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{i,q^{\prime},k})}+\left\|(D\Phi_{(i,k)})^{-1}-{\rm Id}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{i,q^{\prime},k})}\leq\tau_{q^{\prime}}^{-1}\Gamma_{q^{\prime}}^{-5}\mu_{q^{\prime}}\,,

(5.4a)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}\Phi_{(i,k)}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{i,q^{\prime},k})}+\left\|D^{N}\Phi^{-1}_{(i,k)}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{i,q^{\prime},k})}+\left\|D^{N-1}\left((D\Phi_{(i,k)})^{-1}\right)\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{i,q^{\prime},k})}\leq\frac{\mu_{q^{\prime}}(\lambda_{q^{\prime}}\Gamma_{q^{\prime}})^{N-1}}{\tau_{q^{\prime}}\Gamma_{q^{\prime}}^{5}}\,.

(5.4b)

Furthermore, we have the following bounds for
1\leq N+M\leq\nicefrac{{3\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{2}}
and
0\leq N^{\prime}\leq N
:

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N-N^{\prime}}D_{t,q^{\prime}}^{M}D^{N^{\prime}+1}\Phi_{(i,k)}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{i,q^{\prime},k})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mu_{q^{\prime}}(\lambda_{q^{\prime}}\Gamma_{q^{\prime}})^{N}}{\tau_{q^{\prime}}\Gamma_{q^{\prime}}^{5}}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q^{\prime}}^{i}\mu_{q^{\prime}}^{-1},\mathrm{T}_{q^{\prime}-1}^{-1}\Gamma_{q^{\prime}-1}\right)

(5.5a)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N-N^{\prime}}D_{t,q^{\prime}}^{M}D^{N^{\prime}}(D\Phi_{(i,k)})^{-1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{i,q^{\prime},k})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mu_{q^{\prime}}(\lambda_{q^{\prime}}\Gamma_{q^{\prime}})^{N}}{\tau_{q^{\prime}}\Gamma_{q^{\prime}}^{5}}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q^{\prime}}^{i}\mu_{q^{\prime}}^{-1},{\mathrm{T}}_{q^{\prime}-1}^{-1}\Gamma_{q^{\prime}-1}\right)

(5.5b)

Proof.

The proofs of (
5.4a
)–(
5.4b
) are nearly identical to those in
[
6
, Corollary 6.27]
. The estimates come from multiplying the size of
\nabla\widehat{u}_{q^{\prime}}
given in (
2.24
) on the support of
\Omega_{i,q^{\prime},k}
by the length of the timescale from (
5.2
), and paying the derivative cost of
\nabla\widehat{u}_{q^{\prime}}
for each additional spatial derivative past the first. Using Lemma
5.1
, Corollary
5.2
, (
2.24
) at level
q^{\prime}
, and (
2.6a
), we obtain (
5.4a
)–(
5.4b
). Higher order derivatives are similar, and we omit further details.
∎

5.1.4 Stress cutoffs

In this subsection, we introduce the cutoff functions for the stress error
R_{\ell}
obtained from mollifying
R_{q}^{q}
in Lemma
4.1
. The definition is essentially a level-set chopping of
R_{\ell}
. Since
R_{\ell}
satisfies estimates which are totally analogous to
\pi_{\ell}
in
[
29
, Section 5.3]
or
R_{\ell}
in
[
45
, Section 5.3]
, we omit the details in the proofs of the following estimates.

Lemma 5.5

(
Stress cutoffs).

There exist cutoff functions
\{\omega_{j,q}\}_{j\geq 0}
satisfying the following.

(i)

We have that

\displaystyle\sum_{j\geq 0}\omega_{j,q}^{2}(t,x)\equiv 1\,,\qquad\omega_{j,q}\omega_{j^{\prime},q}\equiv 0\quad\textnormal{if}\quad|j-j^{\prime}|>1\,.

(5.6)

(ii)

We have that for
N+M\leq\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}
and any
j\geq 0
,

\displaystyle{\bf 1}_{\mathrm{supp\,}(\omega_{j,q}\psi_{i,q})}|D^{k}D_{t,q}^{m}R_{\ell}(x,t)|

\displaystyle\leq\Gamma_{q}^{2j+6}\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}}(\Gamma_{q}\Lambda_{q})^{k}\mathcal{M}\left(m,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q}^{i}\mu_{q}^{-1},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\right)\,.

(5.7a)

\displaystyle\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}}\Gamma_{q}^{2j}

\displaystyle\leq{\bf 1}_{\mathrm{supp\,}(\omega_{j,q})}|R_{\ell}|

(5.7b)

(iii)

There exists
{j_{\rm max}}(q)\leq\inf\{j\,:\,\frac{1}{4}\Gamma_{q}^{2j}\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}}\geq\Gamma_{q}^{3+\mathsf{C_{\infty}}}\}
, bounded independently of
q
, such that
\omega_{j,q}\equiv 0
for all
\qquad j>{j_{\rm max}}={j_{\rm max}}(q)
. Moreover, we have the bound
\Gamma_{q}^{2j_{\rm max}}\leq\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{\mathsf{C_{\infty}}+6}
.

(iv)

For
q\geq 0
,
0\leq i\leq{i_{\rm max}}
,
0\leq j\leq{j_{\rm max}}
, and
N+M\leq\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{{\bf 1}_{\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,q}}|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\omega_{j,q}|}{\omega_{j,q}^{1-(N+M)/\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}\lesssim(\Gamma_{q}^{5}\Lambda_{q})^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q}^{i+4}\mu_{q}^{-1},{\mathrm{T}}_{q}^{-1}\right)\,.

(5.8)

(v)

For any
r\geq 1
and
0\leq j\leq{j_{\rm max}}
, we have that

\displaystyle\left\|\omega_{j,q}\right\|_{L^{r}}\lesssim\Gamma_{q}^{\frac{2(1-j)}{r}}\,.

(5.9)

5.1.5 Anisotropic checkerboard cutoffs

We recall from
[
29
, subsection 5.4]
the anisotropic cutoffs. Aside from changing
\tau
to
\mu
, these cutoffs are identical to those in
[
29
]
.

Lemma 5.6

(
Mildly anistropic checkerboard cutoffs).

Given
q
,
\xi\in\Xi_{0},\cdots,\Xi_{{\bar{n}}-1}
or
\xi\in\{h_{0},\cdots,h_{{\bar{n}}-1}\}
,
i\leq{i_{\rm max}}
, and
k\in\mathbb{Z}
, there exist cutoff functions
\zeta_{q,i,k,{\xi},\vec{l}}\,(x,t)=\mathcal{X}_{q,\xi,\vec{l}}\left(\Phi_{i,k,q}(x,t)\right)
which satisfy the following properties.

(i)

The support of
\mathcal{X}_{q,\xi,\vec{l}}
is contained in a rectangular prism of dimensions no larger than
\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}}\lambda_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{-8}
in the direction of
\xi_{z}
, and
\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}}\Gamma_{q}^{5}(\lambda_{q+1})^{-1}
in the directions perpendicular to
\xi_{z}
.

(ii)

The material derivative
D_{t,q}(\zeta_{q,i,k,{\xi},\vec{l}})
vanishes.

(iii)

We have the summability property for all
(x,t)\in\mathbb{T}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}

\displaystyle\sum_{\vec{l}}\bigl(\zeta_{q,i,k,{\xi},\vec{l}}\,(x,t)\bigr)^{2}

\displaystyle\equiv 1\,.

(5.10a)

(iv)

Let
A=(\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)})^{-1}
. Then for all
N_{1}+N_{2}+M\leq\nicefrac{{3\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{2}}+1
,

\displaystyle\bigl\|D^{N_{1}}D_{t,q}^{M}({\xi^{\ell}A_{\ell}^{j}\partial_{j}})^{N_{2}}\zeta_{q,i,k,\xi,\vec{l}}\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,q}\widetilde{\chi}_{i,k,q}\right)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\Gamma_{q}^{-5}\lambda_{q+1}\right)^{N_{1}}\left(\Gamma_{q}^{8}\lambda_{q}\right)^{N_{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q}^{i}\mu_{q}^{-1},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{-1}\right)\,.

(5.11)

Lemma 5.7

(
Strongly anisotropic checkerboard cutoff function).

The cutoff functions
\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}
defined using
\boldsymbol{\zeta}
from Definition
4.10, []
satisfy the following properties:

(1)

The material derivative
D_{t,q}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)})
vanishes.

(2)

For all
q,i,k,\xi
,
t\in\mathbb{R}
, and all
x\in\mathbb{T}^{3}
,
\sum_{I}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)})^{2}(x,t)=1
.

(3)

Let
A=(\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)})^{-1}
. Then for all
N_{1}+N_{2}+M\leq\nicefrac{{3\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{2}}+1
, we have that

\displaystyle\bigl\|D^{N_{1}}D_{t,q}^{M}({\xi^{\ell}A_{\ell}^{j}\partial_{j}})^{N_{2}}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,q}\widetilde{\chi}_{i,k,q}\right)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda_{q+\nicefrac{{{\bar{n}}}}{{2}}}^{N_{1}}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q}^{i}\mu_{q}^{-1},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{-1}\right)\,.

(4)

For fixed
q,i,k,\xi
, we have that
\#\left\{(\vec{l},I):\mathrm{supp\,}\left(\zeta_{q,i,k,\xi,\vec{l}}\,\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{I}_{\xi}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\right)\neq\emptyset\right\}\lesssim\Gamma_{q}^{8}\lambda_{q}\lambda_{q+\nicefrac{{{\bar{n}}}}{{2}}}^{2}
.

5.1.6 Cumulative cutoff function

We introduce the cumulative cutoff functions

\displaystyle\eta_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l}}\,(x,t)

\displaystyle:=\psi_{i,q}(x,t)\omega_{j,q}(x,t)\chi_{i,k,q}(t)\zeta_{q,i,k,\xi,\vec{l}}\,(x,t)\,,\qquad\xi\in\cup_{j=0}^{{\bar{n}}-1}\Xi_{j}\,,

(5.12a)

\displaystyle\eta_{i,k,\xi,\vec{l}}

\displaystyle:=\psi_{i,q}\chi_{i,k,q}(t)\zeta_{q,i,k,\vec{\xi},\vec{l}}\,(x,t)\,,\qquad\xi\in\{\vec{h}_{0},\dots\vec{h}_{{\bar{n}}-1}\}\,.

(5.12b)

We have the following
L^{p}
estimates, which may be proven directly using (
5.9
) and (
2.15
).

Lemma 5.8

(
Cumulative support bounds for cutoff functions).

For
r_{1},r_{2}\in[1,\infty]
with
\frac{1}{r_{1}}+\frac{1}{r_{2}}=1
and any
0\leq i\leq{i_{\rm max}}
,
0\leq j,\leq{j_{\rm max}}
, we have that for each
t
,

\displaystyle\sum_{\vec{l}}\left|\mathrm{supp\,}_{x}\left(\eta_{i,k,\vec{e}_{2},\vec{l}}(t,x)\right)\right|+\sum_{\vec{l}}\left|\mathrm{supp\,}_{x}\left(\eta_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l}}(t,x)\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q}^{\frac{-2i+{\mathsf{C}_{b}}}{r_{1}}+\frac{-2j}{r_{2}}+3}\,.

(5.13)

We furthermore have that

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I}\left(\mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{supp\,}\eta_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l}}\boldsymbol{\rho}_{(\xi)}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I}}+\mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{supp\,}\eta_{i,k,\xi,\vec{l}}\boldsymbol{\rho}_{(\xi)}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I}}\right)\approx\sum_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l}}\left(\mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{supp\,}\eta_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l}}\boldsymbol{\rho}_{(\xi)}}+\mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{supp\,}\eta_{i,k,\xi,\vec{l}}\boldsymbol{\rho}_{(\xi)}}\right)\lesssim 1\,.

(5.14)

5.1.7 Cutoff aggregation lemmas

We now recall “aggregation lemmas” from
[
29
, Section 5.6]
, which allow us to sum over the indices in the cumulative cutoffs to produce useful global bounds in Lebesgue spaces. Slight changes been made since
L^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}
bounds on stresses and
L^{3}
bounds on velocities have been replaced with
L^{1}
bounds on stresses and
L^{2}
bounds on velocities, c.f.
\theta\in(0,2]
rather than
(0,3]
. For concision we state the lemmas for the cutoffs from
5.12a
; for the cutoffs from
5.12b
identical statements hold with
\theta_{2}=0
since these cutoffs do not depend on
j
.

Corollary 5.9

(
Aggregated
L^{p}
estimates).

Let
\theta\in(0,2]
, and
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\geq 0
with
\theta_{1}+\theta_{2}=\theta=\nicefrac{{2}}{{p}}
. Let
H=H_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l}}
or
H=H_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I}
be a function with

\displaystyle\mathrm{supp\,}H_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l}}\subseteq\mathrm{supp\,}\eta_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l}}\qquad\textnormal{or}\qquad\mathrm{supp\,}H_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I}\subseteq\mathrm{supp\,}\eta_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l}}\,\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\,.

(5.15)

Assume that there exists
\mathcal{C}_{H},N_{*},M_{*},N_{x},M_{t}
and
\lambda,\Lambda,\tau,\mathrm{T}
such that

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}H_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l}}\right\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left(\left|\mathrm{supp\,}_{x}\left(\eta_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l}}(t,x)\right)\right|^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\mathcal{C}_{H}\Gamma_{q}^{\theta_{1}i+\theta_{2}j}\mathcal{M}\left(N,N_{x},\lambda,\Lambda\right)\mathcal{M}\left(M,M_{t},\tau^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i},\mathrm{T}^{-1}\right)

(5.16a)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}H_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I}\right\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left(\left|\mathrm{supp\,}_{x}\left(\eta_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l}}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}(t,x)\right)\right|^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\mathcal{C}_{H}\Gamma_{q}^{\theta_{1}i+\theta_{2}j}\mathcal{M}\left(N,N_{x},\lambda,\Lambda\right)\mathcal{M}\left(M,M_{t},\tau^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i},\mathrm{T}^{-1}\right)\,

(5.16b)

for
N\leq N_{*},M\leq M_{*}
. Then in the same range of
N
and
M
,

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{i,q}\sum_{i^{\prime},j,k,\xi,\vec{l}}D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}H_{i^{\prime},j,k,\xi,\vec{l}}\right\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q}^{{3+\theta_{1}{\mathsf{C}_{b}}}}\mathcal{C}_{H}\mathcal{M}\left(N,N_{x},\lambda,\Lambda\right)\mathcal{M}\left(M,M_{t},\tau^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+1},\mathrm{T}^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{i,q}\sum_{i^{\prime},j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I}D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}H_{i^{\prime},j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I}\right\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q}^{{3+\theta_{1}{\mathsf{C}_{b}}}}\mathcal{C}_{H}\mathcal{M}\left(N,N_{x},\lambda,\Lambda\right)\mathcal{M}\left(M,M_{t},\tau^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+1},\mathrm{T}^{-1}\right)\,.

5.2 Construction of the Euler-Reynolds perturbation

In this subsection, we define the Euler-Reynolds velocity increment, except for the choice of placement (see subsection
5.5
). None of the discussion in this subsection depend on the choice of placement. For fixed of
i
,
k
, define

\displaystyle R_{q,i,j,k}=\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}\left(e_{q+1}\Gamma_{q}^{2j}\mathrm{Id}-R_{\ell}\right)\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{T}\,,

(5.18)

For all
\xi\in\Xi_{q\,\rm{mod}\,{\bar{n}}}
, we define the coefficient function
a_{\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l}}
by

\displaystyle a_{\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l}}

\displaystyle=a_{(\xi)}=e_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Gamma^{j}_{q}\eta_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l}}\,\gamma_{\xi}\left(\frac{R_{q,i,j,k}}{e_{q+1}\Gamma_{q}^{2j}}\right)

(5.19)

where
\gamma_{\xi}
is defined in Proposition
4.1, []
and

\displaystyle e_{q+1}:=e_{q+1}(t)=\left(\frac{2e(t)-\|u_{q}\|_{2}^{2}-\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}+1}}{3\sum_{j}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\omega_{j,q}^{2}\Gamma_{q}^{2j}}\right)\,.

In order to show that (
5.19
) is well-defined, we recall
(
5.7b
),
(
5.6
), and
(
4.2a
) to get

\displaystyle\sum_{j^{\prime}\geq 0}\int\omega_{j^{\prime},q}^{2}\Gamma_{q}^{2j^{\prime}}\leq 4\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{-1}\int\sum_{j^{\prime}\geq 0}\omega_{j^{\prime},q}^{2}|R_{\ell}|\lesssim\Gamma_{q}^{2}

and further use (
5.4a
), (
5.7a
),
and (
2.28
) to see that for all
j
,

\left|\left({e_{q+1}\Gamma_{q}^{2j}}\right)^{-1}{R_{q,i,j,k}|_{\mathrm{supp\,}\omega_{j,q}}}-\mathrm{Id}\right|\leq\Gamma_{q}^{-1}\,.

(5.20)

Also, by the inductive assumption (
2.28
), the function
(2e(t)-\|u_{q}\|_{2}^{2}-\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}+1})^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}
is positive and smooth, and is of order
\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}
.
The coefficient function
a_{(\xi)}
is then multiplied by an intermittent pipe bundle
\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1}\mathbb{B}_{(\xi)}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}
, where we have used Proposition
4.5, []
(with
\lambda=\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}
and
r=r_{q}
), Definition
3.2
, and the shorthand notation

\displaystyle\mathbb{B}_{(\xi)}=\boldsymbol{\rho}_{(\xi)}\sum_{I}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I}\mathbb{W}_{(\xi)}^{I}

(5.21)

to refer to the pipe bundle associated with the region
\Omega_{0}=\mathrm{supp\,}\zeta_{q,i,k,\xi,\vec{l}}\cap\{t=k\mu_{q}\Gamma_{q}^{-i-2}\}
and the index
j
. We will use
\mathbb{U}_{(\xi)}^{I}
to denote the potential satisfying
\mathrm{curl\,}\mathbb{U}_{(\xi)}^{I}=\mathbb{W}_{(\xi)}^{I}
. Applying Proposition
4.5, []
, we define the principal part of the Reynolds corrector by

w_{q+1,R}^{(p)}=\sum_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I}\underbrace{a_{(\xi)}\left(\boldsymbol{\rho}_{(\xi)}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I}\right)\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\mathrm{curl\,}\left(\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{T}\mathbb{U}_{(\xi)}^{I}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\right)}_{=:w_{(\xi)}^{(p),I}}\,.

(5.22)

The notation
w_{(\xi)}^{(p),I}
refers to
fixed
values of
i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I
. We add the divergence corrector

w_{q+1,R}^{(c)}=\sum_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I}\underbrace{\nabla\left(a_{(\xi)}\left(\boldsymbol{\rho}_{(\xi)}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I}\right)\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\right)\times\left(\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{T}\mathbb{U}_{(\xi)}^{I}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\right)}_{=:w_{(\xi)}^{(c),I}}\,,

(5.23)

so that the mean-zero, divergence-free total Reynolds corrector is given by

w_{q+1,R}=\sum_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I}\underbrace{\mathrm{curl\,}\left(a_{(\xi)}\left(\boldsymbol{\rho}_{(\xi)}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I}\right)\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{T}\mathbb{U}_{(\xi)}^{I}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\right)}_{=:w_{(\xi)}^{I}}\,.

(5.24)

5.3 Construction of helical perturbation

In this subsection, we define the helical velocity increment, except for the choice of placement. Define
h_{q+1}^{2}
by

h_{q+1}^{2}=|\mathbb{T}^{3}|^{-1}(h(t)-H(u_{q}))\left(1-\Gamma_{q-1}(2\Gamma_{q})^{-1}\right)\,,

(5.25)

Note that the right hand side of (
5.25
) is strictly positive due to the inductive assumption (
2.30
), so that
h_{q+1}
is well-defined. For
\xi=\vec{h}_{q\,\rm{mod}\,{\bar{n}}}
, we define the coefficient function
a_{\xi,\pm,i,k,\vec{l}}
by

\displaystyle a_{\xi,i,k,\vec{l}}=a_{{(\xi)}}=\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}\lambda_{q*}^{-\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}\eta_{i,k,\xi,\vec{l}}\,h_{q+1}\,.

(5.26)

The coefficient function
a_{(\xi)}
is then multiplied by a helical pipe bundle
\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1}\mathbb{B}_{(\xi)}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}
, where we have used Proposition
3.3
and the shorthand notation

\mathbb{B}_{(\xi)}=\boldsymbol{\rho}_{(\xi)}\sum_{I}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I}\mathbb{H}^{I}_{(\xi)}

(5.27)

to refer to the helical pipe bundle associated with the region
\Omega_{0}=\mathrm{supp\,}\zeta_{q,i,k,\xi,\vec{l}}\cap\{t=k\mu_{q}\Gamma_{q}^{-i-2}\}
. We will use
\widetilde{}\mathbb{U}_{(\xi)}^{I}
to denote the potential satisfying
\mathrm{curl\,}\widetilde{}\mathbb{U}_{(\xi)}^{I}=\mathbb{H}_{(\xi)}^{I}
. So, from (
3.11
), we have

\widetilde{}\mathbb{U}_{(\xi)}^{I}=(\nabla\Psi_{q,\xi}^{+})^{T}\mathbb{U}_{(\xi)}^{I}\circ\Psi_{q,\xi}^{+}\,.

(5.28)

Applying (
3.16
), we define the principal part of the helicity corrector by

\displaystyle w_{q+1,h}^{(p)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,k,\vec{l},I}\underbrace{a_{(\xi)}\left(\boldsymbol{\rho}_{(\xi)}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I}\right)\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\mathrm{curl\,}\left(\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{T}\widetilde{}\mathbb{U}_{(\xi)}^{I}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\right)}_{w_{(\xi)}^{(p),I}}\,.

(5.29)

The notation
w_{(\xi)}^{(p),I}
refers to fixed values of
i,k,\vec{l},I
; note that
\xi
is always equal to
\vec{h}_{(q+1)\text{ mod }{\bar{n}}}
. We add the divergence corrector
w_{q+1,h}^{(c)}
by

\displaystyle w_{q+1,h}^{(c)}=\sum_{i,k,\vec{l},I}\underbrace{\nabla\left(a_{(\xi)}\left(\boldsymbol{\rho}_{(\xi)}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I}\right)\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\right)\times\left(\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{T}\widetilde{}\mathbb{U}_{(\xi)}^{I}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\right)}_{w_{(\xi)}^{(hc),I}}\,,

(5.30)

so that the mean-zero, divergence-free total helicity corrector is given by

\displaystyle w_{q+1,h}

\displaystyle=w_{q+1,h}^{(p)}+w_{q+1,h}^{(c)}=\sum_{i,k,\vec{l},I}\underbrace{\mathrm{curl\,}\left(a_{(\xi)}\left(\boldsymbol{\rho}_{(\xi)}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I}\right)\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{T}\widetilde{}\mathbb{U}_{(\xi)}^{I}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\right)}_{w_{(\xi)}^{(h),I}}\,.

(5.31)

5.4 Definition of the complete corrector

We set

\displaystyle w_{q+1}=w_{q+1,R}+w_{q+1,h}\,,\qquad w_{q+1}^{(p)}:=w_{q+1,R}^{(p)}+w_{q+1,h}^{(p)}\,,\qquad w_{q+1}^{(c)}:=w_{q+1,R}^{(c)}+w_{q+1,h}^{(c)}\,.

(5.32)

With
\diamond=R,h
to distinguish between Reynolds and helicity correctors, we shall use the notations
w_{q+1,\diamond}
,
w_{q+1,\diamond}^{(p)}
, and
w_{q+1,\diamond}^{(c)}
.

5.5 Dodging for the velocity increment

In this section, we define a mollified velocity increment
\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}
. We then introduce Lemma
5.11
, which is slighter stronger than Hypothesis
2.3
.

Definition 5.10

(
Definition of
\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}
and
u_{q+1}
).

Let
\mathcal{\widetilde{P}}_{q+{\bar{n}},x,t}
denote a space-time mollifier which is a product of compactly supported kernels at spatial scale
\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}-1}^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}
and temporal scale
\mathrm{T}_{q+1}^{-1}
, where both kernels have vanishing moments up to
10\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}
and are
C^{10\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}
differentiable. Define

\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}:=\mathcal{\widetilde{P}}_{q+{\bar{n}},x,t}w_{q+1}\,,\qquad u_{q+1}=u_{q}+\widehat{w}_{{q+{\bar{n}}}}\,.

(5.33)

Recalling from (
2.7
) the notations
B(\Omega,\lambda^{-1})
and
B(\Omega,\lambda^{-1},\tau)
, we have that

\mathrm{supp\,}\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}\subseteq B\left(\mathrm{supp\,}w_{q+1},\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{-1},\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\mathrm{T}_{q}\right)\,.

(5.34)

Then using (
3.3
), (
5.21
), (
3.9
), (
3.18
), (
5.21
), and (
5.29
), we set

\displaystyle\varrho_{(\xi),R}^{I}:=\xi\cdot\mathbb{W}_{(\xi)}^{I}\,,\qquad\varrho_{(\xi),h}^{I}(\cdot):=\left[\left(\nabla\Psi_{q}^{\pm}\right)^{-1}\xi\right]\cdot\mathbb{H}_{{(\xi)}}^{I}\,.

(5.35)

Next, in slight conflict with (
2.7
), we shall also use the notation

\displaystyle B\left(\mathrm{supp\,}\varrho_{{(\xi)},\diamond}^{I},\lambda^{-1}\right):=\left\{x\in\mathbb{T}^{3}\,:\,\exists y\in\mathrm{supp\,}\varrho_{{(\xi)},\diamond}^{I}\,,|x-y|\leq\lambda^{-1}\right\}

(5.36)

throughout this section, despite the fact that
\mathrm{supp\,}\varrho_{{(\xi)},\diamond}^{I}
is not a set in space-time, but merely a set in space. We shall also use the same notation but with
\varrho_{{(\xi)},\diamond}^{I}
replaced by
\boldsymbol{\rho}_{\xi}^{\diamond}
. Finally, for any smooth set
\Omega\subseteq\mathbb{T}^{3}
and any flow map
\Phi
defined in Definition
5.3
, we use the notation

\Omega\circ\Phi:=\left\{(y,t):t\in\mathbb{R},\Phi(y,t)\in\Omega\right\}=\mathrm{supp\,}\left(\mathbf{1}_{\Omega}\circ\Phi\right)\,.

(5.37)

In other words,
\Omega\circ\Phi
is a space-time set whose characteristic function is annihilated by
D_{t,q}
.

Lemma 5.11

(
Dodging for
w_{q+1}
and
\widehat{w}_{{q+{\bar{n}}}}
).

We construct
w_{q+1}
so that the following hold.

(i)

Let
q+1\leq q^{\prime}\leq q+\nicefrac{{{\bar{n}}}}{{2}}
and fix indices
\diamond,i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l}
, which we abbreviate by
({(\xi)},\diamond)
, for a coefficient function
a_{{(\xi)},\diamond}
(c.f. (
5.19
), (
5.26
)). Then

B\left(\mathrm{supp\,}\widehat{w}_{q^{\prime}},\frac{1}{2}{\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{2}},{2\mathrm{T}_{q}}\right)\cap\mathrm{supp\,}\left(\widetilde{\chi}_{i,k,q}\zeta_{q,\diamond,i,k,\xi,\vec{l}}\,\boldsymbol{\rho}_{{(\xi)}}^{\diamond}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\right)=\emptyset\,.

(5.38)

(ii)

Let
q^{\prime}
satisfy
q+1\leq q^{\prime}\leq q+{\bar{n}}-1
, fix indices
({(\xi)},\diamond,I)
, and assume that
\Phi_{(i,k)}
is the identity at time
t_{{(\xi)}}
, cf. Definition
5.3
. Then we have that

\displaystyle B\left(\mathrm{supp\,}\widehat{w}_{q^{\prime}},\frac{1}{4}\lambda_{q^{\prime}}^{-1}\Gamma_{q^{\prime}}^{2},2\mathrm{T}_{q}\right)\cap\mathrm{supp\,}

\displaystyle\left(\widetilde{\chi}_{i,k,q}\zeta_{q,\diamond,i,k,\xi,\vec{l}}\left(\boldsymbol{\rho}_{(\xi)}^{\diamond}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I,\diamond}\right)\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\right)

\displaystyle\cap B\left(\mathrm{supp\,}\varrho^{I}_{(\xi),\diamond},\frac{1}{2}{\lambda_{q^{\prime}}^{-1}\Gamma_{q^{\prime}}^{2}}\right)\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}=\emptyset\,.

(5.39)

As a consequence we have

B\left(\mathrm{supp\,}\widehat{w}_{q^{\prime}},\frac{1}{4}{\lambda_{q^{\prime}}^{-1}\Gamma_{q^{\prime}}^{2}},2\mathrm{T}_{q}\right)\cap\mathrm{supp\,}w_{q+1}=\emptyset\,.

(5.40)

(iii)

Consider the set of indices
\{({(\xi)},\diamond,I)\}
, whose elements we use to index the correctors constructed in (
5.24
) and (
5.31
), and let
\texttt{l},\overline{\texttt{l}}\in\{p,c,hp,hc\}
denote either principal or divergence corrector parts for Euler-Reynolds and helicity correctors, respectively. Then if
(\overline{\diamond},(\overline{\xi}),\overline{I})\neq(\diamond,(\xi),I)
, we have that for any
\texttt{l},\overline{\texttt{l}}
,

B\left(\mathrm{supp\,}w_{{(\xi)}}^{(\texttt{l}),I},\frac{1}{4}\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{2}\right)\cap B\left(\mathrm{supp\,}w_{(\overline{\xi})}^{(\overline{\texttt{l}}),\overline{I}},\frac{1}{4}\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{2}\right)=\emptyset\,.

(5.41)

(iv)

\widehat{w}_{{q+{\bar{n}}},R}
satisfies Hypothesis
2.4
with
q
replaced by
q+1
.

Proof.

The proof is the same as
[
30
, Lemma 4.5]
. The role played by the Euler-Reynolds corrector when
\diamond=R
is identical in both cases, and the role played by the current corrector when
\diamond=\varphi
in
[
30
]
is analogous to that of the case
\diamond=h
here. Of crucial importance is the fact that the helical deformations defined in Proposition
3.3
are small enough that the helically deformed pipe flow fits in a pipe flow with radius enlarged by only a constant,
q
-independent factor; see (
3.18
). Therefore, the same dodging procedure which works for “straight pipes,” i.e. those which are initiated without helical deformations, works for the helical pipe bundles as well. Finally, we note that the proof of the dodging (see
[
30
, Lemmas 4.1, 4.2]
) requires velocity increments at different generations in
q
to be defined using different vector directions. This condition is satisfied due to Definition
3.1
and the choices of
\xi
in (
5.19
) and (
5.26
). We omit further details.
∎

Remark 5.12

(
Verifying Hypothesis
2.3
).

We claim that (
5.40
) and (
5.34
) imply that Hypothesis
2.3
. The proof is identical to that in
[
29
, Remark 6.3]
, and so we omit further details.

5.6 Estimates for
w_{q+1}
and
\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}

We now record the needed estimates for the velocity increments. As the proofs are nearly identical to those in
[
29
]
, we omit most details.

Lemma 5.13

(
Coefficient function estimates
)
.

Let
A=(\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)})^{-1}
. For
N,N^{\prime},N^{\prime\prime},M
with
N^{\prime\prime},N^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}
and
N,M\leq{\nicefrac{{\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{3}}}
, and
r\in[1,\infty]
we have the following estimates.

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N-N^{\prime\prime}}D_{t,q}^{M}(\xi^{\ell}A_{\ell}^{h}\partial_{h})^{N^{\prime}}D^{N^{\prime\prime}}a_{\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l}}\right\|_{r}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\mathrm{supp\,}\eta_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l}}\right|^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{r}}}\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Gamma^{j+{7}}_{q}\left({\Gamma_{q}^{-5}\lambda_{q+1}}\right)^{N}\left({\Gamma_{q}^{5}\Lambda_{q}}\right)^{N^{\prime}}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\mu_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+13},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\right)\,,

(5.42a)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N-N^{\prime\prime}}D_{t,q}^{M}(\xi^{\ell}A_{\ell}^{h}\partial_{h})^{N^{\prime}}D^{N^{\prime\prime}}\left(a_{\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l}}\left(\boldsymbol{\rho}_{(\xi)}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I}\right)\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\right)\right\|_{r}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\mathrm{supp\,}\left(\eta_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l}}\,\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I}\right)\right|^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{r}}}\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Gamma_{q}^{j{+10}}\left({\lambda_{q+\lfloor\nicefrac{{{\bar{n}}}}{{2}}\rfloor}}\right)^{N}\left({\Gamma_{q}^{5}\Lambda_{q}}\right)^{N^{\prime}}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\mu_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+13},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\right)\,,

(5.42b)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N-N^{\prime\prime}}D_{t,q}^{M}(\xi^{\ell}A_{\ell}^{h}\partial_{h})^{N^{\prime}}D^{N^{\prime\prime}}a_{\xi,i,k,\vec{l}}\right\|_{r}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}}{\lambda_{q*}^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}}{\Gamma_{q-1}^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\left|\mathrm{supp\,}\eta_{i,k,\xi,\vec{l}}\right|^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{r}}}\left({\Gamma_{q}^{-5}\lambda_{q+1}}\right)^{N}\left({\Gamma_{q}^{{13}}\Lambda_{q}}\right)^{N^{\prime}}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\mu_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+{13}},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}{\Gamma_{q}^{8}}\right)\,,

(5.42c)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N-N^{\prime\prime}}D_{t,q}^{M}(\xi^{\ell}A_{\ell}^{h}\partial_{h})^{N^{\prime}}D^{N^{\prime\prime}}\left(a_{\xi,i,k,\vec{l}}\left(\boldsymbol{\rho}_{(\xi)}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I}\right)\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\right)\right\|_{r}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}}{\lambda_{q*}^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}}{\Gamma_{q-1}^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\left|\mathrm{supp\,}\eta_{i,k,\xi,\vec{l}}\,\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I}\right|^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{r}}}\left({\lambda_{q+\lfloor\nicefrac{{{\bar{n}}}}{{2}}\rfloor}}\right)^{N}\left({\Gamma_{q}^{{13}}\Lambda_{q}}\right)^{N^{\prime}}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\mu_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+{13}},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}{\Gamma_{q}^{8}}\right)\,.

(5.42d)

In the case that
r=\infty
, the above estimates give that

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N-N^{\prime\prime}}D_{t,q}^{M}(\xi^{\ell}A_{\ell}^{h}\partial_{h})^{N^{\prime}}D^{N^{\prime\prime}}a_{\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l}}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim{\Gamma_{q}^{\frac{\mathsf{C_{\infty}}}{2}+{10}}}\left(\Gamma_{q}^{-5}\lambda_{q+1}\right)^{N}\left({\Gamma_{q}^{{13}}\Lambda_{q}}\right)^{N^{\prime}}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\mu_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+{13}},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}{\Gamma_{q}^{8}}\right)\,,

(5.43a)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N-N^{\prime\prime}}D_{t,q}^{M}(\xi^{\ell}A_{\ell}^{h}\partial_{h})^{N^{\prime}}D^{N^{\prime\prime}}a_{\xi,i,k,\vec{l}}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}\lambda_{q*}^{-\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}{\Gamma_{q-1}^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\left(\Gamma_{q}^{-5}\lambda_{q+1}\right)^{N}\left({\Gamma_{q}^{8}\Lambda_{q}}\right)^{N^{\prime}}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\mu_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+13},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\right)\,,

(5.43b)

with analogous estimates (incorporating a loss of
\Gamma_{q}^{3}
for
\diamond=R
) holding for the product
a_{(\xi),\diamond}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I}\boldsymbol{\rho}_{(\xi)}
. The constants in the above estimates (
5.42
)-(
5.43
) depend on the maximum values of the first
\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}
derivatives of
e(t)
and
h(t)
on the time interval
[-1,T+1]
; crucially, they are independent of
q
.

Proof.

The proofs of these statements are nearly identical to those from
[
29
, Lemma 6.4]
. The only differences are as follows. First, there are no factors of
r_{q}^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{3}}}
, which are not needed since we aim for
L^{2}
regularity rather than
L^{3}
regularity. Second, the amplitude for the helical coefficient functions has no
j
dependence. Finally, we estimate the terms when the material derivative falls on
H(u_{q})
in the coefficient of the helical perturbation by using the definition (
5.25
), the inductive assumptions (
2.34
) and (
2.30
), and the computation

\displaystyle 2

\displaystyle\left((h-H)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\right)\left[\nicefrac{{d}}{{dt}}\right]^{N+1}\left((h-H)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\left[\nicefrac{{d}}{{dt}}\right]^{N+1}\left(h-H\right)+\sum_{0<N^{\prime}<N+1}c_{N,N^{\prime}}\left[\nicefrac{{d}}{{dt}}\right]^{N^{\prime}}\left((h-H)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\right)\left[\nicefrac{{d}}{{dt}}\right]^{N+1-N^{\prime}}\left((h-H)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\right)\,,

which implies by induction that for
N+1\leq\nicefrac{{\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{3}}
(here we crucially use the lower bound (
2.30
))

\displaystyle\left|\left[\nicefrac{{d}}{{dt}}\right]^{N+1}\left((h-H)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\right)\right|\lesssim\Gamma_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathcal{M}\left(N+1,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\mu_{q-1}^{-1},\mathrm{T}_{q-1}^{-1}\right)\,.

∎

Corollary 5.14

(
Full velocity increment estimates
)
.

For
N,M\leq{\nicefrac{{\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{4}}}
, we have
5
5

5

We have added the subscript
\diamond=R,h
to the notation from (
5.22
) and (
5.29
) since the estimates differ.

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}w_{(\xi),R}^{(p),I}\right\|_{L^{r}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\mathrm{supp\,}\left(\eta_{{(\xi)}}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I}\right)\right|^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{r}}}\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Gamma_{q}^{j+{10}}r_{q}^{\frac{2}{r}-1}\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\mu_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+{13}},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}{\Gamma_{q}^{8}}\right)

(5.44a)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}w_{(\xi),R}^{(p),I}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q}^{\frac{\mathsf{C_{\infty}}}{2}+{13}}r_{q}^{-1}\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\mu_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+{13}},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}{\Gamma_{q}^{8}}\right)\,,

(5.44b)

(5.44c)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}w_{(\xi),h}^{(p),I}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}
