(5.44d)

Also, for
N,M\leq{\nicefrac{{\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{4}}}
, we have that

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}w_{(\xi),R}^{(c),I}\right\|_{L^{r}}

\displaystyle\lesssim r_{q}\left|\mathrm{supp\,}\left(\eta_{{(\xi)}}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I}\right)\right|^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{r}}}\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Gamma_{q}^{j+{10}}r_{q}^{\frac{2}{r}-1}\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\mu_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+{13}},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}{\Gamma_{q}^{8}}\right)

(5.45a)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}w_{(\xi),R}^{(c),I}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q}^{\frac{\mathsf{C_{\infty}}}{2}+{13}}\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\mu_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+{13}},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}{\Gamma_{q}^{8}}\right)\,,

(5.45b)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}w_{(\xi),h}^{(c),I}\right\|_{L^{r}}

\displaystyle\lesssim r_{q}\left|\mathrm{supp\,}\left(\eta_{{(\xi)}}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I}\right)\right|^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{r}}}\frac{\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}}{\lambda_{q*}^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}}{\Gamma_{q-1}^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}r_{q}^{\frac{2}{r}-1}\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\mu_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+{13}},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}{\Gamma_{q}^{8}}\right)

(5.45c)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}w_{(\xi),h}^{(c),I}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}}{\lambda_{q*}^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}}{\Gamma_{q-1}^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\mu_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+{13}},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}{\Gamma_{q}^{8}}\right)\,.

(5.45d)

Proof of Corollary
5.14
.

The proof is essentially identical to
[
29
, Corollary 6.6]
; the main ingredients are the estimates from Lemma
5.13
and the decoupling result from
[
29
, Lemma A.1]
.
∎

Finally, we estimate the mollified velocity increment given in Definition
5.10
.

Lemma 5.15

(
Estimates on
\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}
)
.

We have that
\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}
satisfies the following properties.

(i)

For all
N+M\leq 2\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}
, we have that

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q+{\bar{n}}-1}^{M}\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,q+{\bar{n}}-1})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\Gamma_{q}^{20}\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}+\frac{\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}}{\lambda_{q*}^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}}{\Gamma_{q-1}^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\right)\left(\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}-1}\right)^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}-1}^{i-1}\mu_{q+{\bar{n}}-1}^{-1},\mathrm{T}_{q+{\bar{n}}-1}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}-1}\right)

(5.46a)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q+{\bar{n}}-1}^{M}\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,q+{\bar{n}}-1})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\Gamma_{q}^{\nicefrac{{\mathsf{C_{\infty}}}}{{2}}+16}+\frac{\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}}{\lambda_{q*}^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}}{\Gamma_{q-1}^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\right)r_{q}^{-1}\left(\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}-1}\right)^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}-1}^{i-1}\mu_{q+{\bar{n}}-1}^{-1},\mathrm{T}_{q+{\bar{n}}-1}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}-1}\right)\,.

(5.46b)

(ii)

For all
N+M\leq\nicefrac{{\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{4}}
, we have that

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q+{\bar{n}}-1}^{M}\left(w_{q+1}-\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}\right)\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{q+3{\bar{n}}}^{3}\mathrm{T}_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{25\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}}}\left(\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}-1}\right)^{N}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\mu_{q+{\bar{n}}-1}^{-1},\mathrm{T}_{q+{\bar{n}}-1}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}-1}\right)\,.

(5.47)

Proof of Lemma
5.15
.

The proof of this Lemma is nearly identical to that of
[
29
, Lemma 6.7]
. The main ingredients are the aggregation lemmas, the previous lemmas from this section, a mollification lemma, and parameter inequalities for
\mathrm{T}_{q}
from (
8.7
) and
N_{g}
and
N_{c}
(large integers quantifying how many derivatives are needed to run the abstract mollification lemma) from (
8.10
).
∎

6
The helicity and energy increments

6.1 Helicity increment

In this subsection, we consider the helicity generated by
w_{q+1}
.

Lemma 6.1

(
w^{(p)}_{q+1,h}
reduces the helicity error
)
.

w_{q+1,h}^{(p)}
as defined in (
5.29
) satisfies

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma_{q-1}}{4\Gamma_{q}^{2}}\leq h(t)-H(u_{q})-\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}w_{q+1,h}^{(p)}(t,x)\cdot\mathrm{curl\,}w_{q+1,h}^{(p)}(t,x)\,dx\leq\frac{3}{4}\Gamma_{q}^{-1}\,.

(6.1)

In addition, for any multiplier
\varphi:\mathbb{R}^{3}\rightarrow\overline{B_{1}(0)}\subset\mathbb{C}
,
w_{q+1,h}^{(p)}
satisfies

\left|\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathbb{P}_{\varphi}(w_{q+1,h}^{(p)})\cdot\mathrm{curl\,}w_{q+1,h}^{(p)}\right|<\frac{1}{10}\Gamma_{q-4}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\,.

(6.2)

Finally, for
M\leq\nicefrac{{\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{3}}
,

\left|\left[\nicefrac{{d}}{{dt}}\right]^{M}H(w_{q+1,h}^{(p)})\right|\lesssim\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\mu_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{20},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{8}\right)\,.

(6.3)

Proof.

We break the proof into steps. In
Step 1
, we prove (
6.2
) and (
6.3
) directly from the amplitude estimates. In
Step 2
, we decompose the helicity
H(w_{q+1,h}^{(p)})
into the leading order term and the rest.
In
Step 3
and
Step 4
,
we compute the leading-order and remaining contributions to the helicity, respectively. In
Step 5
, we conclude the proof, obtaining (
6.1
).

For simplicity we perform all computations in the case
\xi=\vec{h}_{0}=\vec{e}_{2}
, which corresponds to the case
q\,\textnormal{mod}\,{\bar{n}}=0
; see (
5.26
). Setting
\widetilde{a}_{(\vec{e}_{2})}:=a_{(\vec{e}_{2})}\left(\boldsymbol{\rho}_{(\vec{e}_{2})}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\vec{e}_{2}}^{I}\right)\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}
and
\widetilde{\Phi}_{(i,k)}=\Psi_{q}^{+}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}
, recall from (
5.29
), (
3.8
), and (
3.16
) that the principal part of the helicity perturbation is given by

\displaystyle w_{q+1,h}^{(p)}=\sum_{i,k,\vec{l},I}\widetilde{a}_{(\vec{e}_{2})}\,\left(\nabla\widetilde{\Phi}_{(i,k)}\right)^{-1}\vec{e}_{2}\varrho_{\vec{e}_{2},\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}},r_{q}}^{k}\circ\widetilde{\Phi}_{(i,k)}\,.

Step 1: Proof of (
6.2
) and (
6.3
).

Since the multiplier operator
\mathbb{P}_{\varphi}
is bounded on
L^{2}
with unit operator norm, we use Corollary
5.14
, (
5.14
), (
2.6b
), and (
8.5f
) to estimate

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathbb{P}_{\varphi}(w_{q+1,h}^{(p)})\cdot\mathrm{curl\,}w_{q+1,h}^{(p)}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|w_{q+1,h}^{(p)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|\mathrm{curl\,}w_{q+1,h}^{(p)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\sum_{i,k,\xi,\vec{l},I}\left|\mathrm{supp\,}\left(\eta_{i,k,\xi,\vec{l}}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I}\right)\right|\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}}{\lambda_{q*}^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}}\Gamma_{q-1}^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\frac{\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{2}}{\lambda_{q*}^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}}\Gamma_{q-1}^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\frac{\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}}{\lambda_{q*}}\right)^{6}\Gamma_{q-1}^{-2}\lesssim\left(\Gamma_{q-1}^{-1}\Gamma_{q-100}^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{100}}}\right)^{2}<\left(\frac{1}{10}\Gamma_{q-4}^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\right)^{2}\,.

This proves (
6.2
). In order to prove (
6.3
), we consider
M\geq 1
, since
M=0
follows from (
6.2
). When
M\geq 1
, we convert time derivatives outside the integral to material derivatives inside the integral, since the material derivative is taken along a divergence-free vector field, obtaining

\displaystyle\left|\left[\nicefrac{{d}}{{dt}}\right]^{M}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}w_{q+1,h}^{(p)}\cdot\mathrm{curl\,}w_{q+1,h}^{(p)}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\sum_{0\leq M^{\prime}\leq M}c_{M,M^{\prime}}D_{t,q}^{M^{\prime}}w_{q+1,h}^{(p)}\cdot D_{t,q}^{M-M^{\prime}}\mathrm{curl\,}w_{q+1,h}^{(p)}\right|\,.

Then using Hölder’s inequality and Corollaries
5.14
and
5.9
gives the desired estimate.

Step 2: Helicity decomposition.
By using the dodging properties between
\{w_{(\vec{e}_{2})}^{(p),I}\}
from Lemma
5.11
and (
5.41
), and denoting
\widetilde{A}=(\nabla\widetilde{\Phi}_{(i,k)})^{-1}
, we decompose

\displaystyle H\left(w_{q+1,h}^{(p)}\right)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}w_{q+1,h}^{(p)}\cdot\mathrm{curl\,}w_{q+1,h}^{(p)}dx=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,k,\vec{l},I\end{subarray}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}w_{(\vec{e}_{2}),h}^{(p),I}\cdot\mathrm{curl\,}w_{(\vec{e}_{2}),h}^{(p),I}dx

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,k,\vec{l},I\end{subarray}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\widetilde{a}_{(\vec{e}_{2})}\widetilde{A}\vec{e}_{2}\varrho_{\vec{e}_{2},\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}},r_{q}}}^{k}(\widetilde{\Phi}_{(i,k)})\cdot\mathrm{curl\,}\left(\widetilde{a}_{(\vec{e}_{2})}\widetilde{A}\vec{e}_{2}\varrho_{\vec{e}_{2},\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}},r_{q}}}^{k}(\widetilde{\Phi}_{(i,k)})\right)dx\,.

Suppressing all indices in the summand for convenience, we decompose the helicity further based on whether curl operator falls on
\widetilde{A}e_{2}
or not:

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,k,\vec{l},I\end{subarray}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\widetilde{a}^{2}{\varrho}(\widetilde{\Phi})\varrho(\widetilde{\Phi})\widetilde{A}\vec{e}_{2}\cdot\mathrm{curl\,}(\widetilde{A}\vec{e}_{2})dx+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\widetilde{a}\varrho(\widetilde{\Phi})\widetilde{A}\vec{e}_{2}\cdot\nabla\left(\widetilde{a}\varrho(\widetilde{\Phi})\right)\times(\widetilde{A}\vec{e}_{2})dx=:H_{1}+H_{2}\,.

(6.4)

Then the second term
H_{2}
vanishes due to the fact that
\nabla\left(\widetilde{a}\varrho(\widetilde{\Phi})\right)\times(\widetilde{A}\vec{e}_{2})
is orthogonal to
\widetilde{A}\vec{e}_{2}
. Lastly, from the proof of Proposition
3.3
, we recall that

\widetilde{e}_{2}\cdot\mathrm{curl\,}\widetilde{e}_{2}=\frac{\lambda_{q*}^{3}}{\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{2}}\,\text{ and }\widetilde{A}\vec{e}_{2}=(\nabla\Phi)^{-1}(\widetilde{e}_{2}\circ\Phi)\,,

and we decompose
H_{1}
into

\displaystyle H_{1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,k,\vec{l},I\end{subarray}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\widetilde{a}^{2}\varrho^{2}(\widetilde{\Phi})\left(\widetilde{A}\vec{e}_{2}\cdot\mathrm{curl\,}(\widetilde{A}\vec{e}_{2})-(\widetilde{e}_{2}\cdot\mathrm{curl\,}\widetilde{e}_{2})(\Phi)\right)dx

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\lambda_{q*}^{3}}{\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\widetilde{a}^{2}((\mathbb{P}_{=0}+\mathbb{P}_{\neq 0})(\varrho^{2}\circ\Psi))(\Phi)dx

\displaystyle=:H_{11}+H_{12}+H_{13}\,.

As we will see, the
H_{12}
will be the leading order term and the rest will be small.

Step 3: Leading-order helicity contribution
H_{12}
.
Using (
5.26
) and the fact that
\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{2}
and
\eta^{2}
are partitions of unity and
\mathbb{P}_{=0}(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{2})=1
, we write

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,k,\vec{l},I\end{subarray}}\frac{\lambda_{q*}^{3}}{\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\widetilde{a}^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{=0}(\varrho^{2}\circ\Psi))(\Phi)dx

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,k,\vec{l},I\end{subarray}}h_{q+1}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\eta^{2}(\boldsymbol{\rho}\boldsymbol{\zeta})^{2}\circ\Phi\,dx=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,k,\vec{l}\end{subarray}}h_{q+1}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\eta^{2}\left(\mathbb{P}_{=0}+\mathbb{P}_{\neq 0}\right)\left(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{2}\right)\circ(\Phi)\,dx

\displaystyle=(h(t)-H(u_{q}))\left(1-\frac{\Gamma_{q-1}}{2\Gamma_{q}}\right)+\sum_{i,k,\vec{l}}h_{q+1}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\eta^{2}(\Phi^{-1})\mathbb{P}_{\neq 0}(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{2})\,dx\,.

(6.5)

We first show that the second term is small. Note that
\boldsymbol{\rho}
is
(\nicefrac{{\mathbb{T}}}{{\lambda_{q+1}\Gamma_{q}^{-4}}})^{3}
-periodic by item
1
in Proposition
4.9, []
and the spatial derivative cost of
\eta^{2}(\Phi^{-1})
is
\lambda_{q+1}\Gamma_{q}^{-5}
from (
5.12b
), (
2.13
), and (
iv
). Therefore this term can be made small after integrating by parts many times. Specifically, using the
L^{1}
norm of the high-frequency function, the
L^{\infty}
norm of the low-frequency object, Lemma
5.6
to bound the number of different values of
\vec{l}
, and (
8.12a
), we bound the second term by

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{i,k,\vec{l}}h_{q+1}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\eta^{2}(\Phi^{-1})\mathbb{P}_{\neq 0}(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{2})\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\sum_{i,k,\vec{l}}h_{q+1}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}(-\Delta)^{\nicefrac{{{\mathsf{d}}}}{{2}}}\eta^{2}(\Phi^{-1})(-\Delta)^{-\nicefrac{{{\mathsf{d}}}}{{2}}}\mathbb{P}_{\geq\lambda_{q+1}\Gamma_{q}^{-4}}(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{2})\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{10}\Gamma_{q}^{-{\mathsf{d}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\leq\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{-1}\,.

(6.6)

Step 4: Analyzing the remaining contributions
{H_{11}}
and
H_{13}
.

We first analyze
H_{11}
using

\displaystyle(\mathrm{curl\,}(\widetilde{A}\vec{e}_{2}))_{l}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon_{lmn}\partial_{m}(\widetilde{A}(\vec{e}_{2}))_{n}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon_{lmn}\partial_{m}((\nabla\Phi)^{-1}_{no}(\widetilde{e}_{2}\circ\Phi)_{o})

\displaystyle=\varepsilon_{lmn}(\partial_{m}(\nabla\Phi)^{-1}_{no})(\widetilde{e}_{2}\circ\Phi)_{o}+\varepsilon_{lmn}(\nabla\Phi)^{-1}_{no}(\nabla\Phi)_{vm}(\partial_{v}(\widetilde{e}_{2})_{o})(\Phi)\,.

Combining this with (
5.4a
)–(
5.5b
), (
3.20
)–(
3.21
), and (
8.5d
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\widetilde{A}\vec{e}_{2}\cdot\mathrm{curl\,}(\widetilde{A}\vec{e}_{2})-(\widetilde{e}_{2}\cdot\mathrm{curl\,}\widetilde{e}_{2})(\Phi)\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}(\widetilde{a}))}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|(\nabla\Phi)^{-1}_{lw}(\widetilde{e}_{2}\circ\Phi)_{w}\varepsilon_{lmn}(\partial_{m}(\nabla\Phi)^{-1}_{no})(\widetilde{e}_{2}\circ\Phi)_{o}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}(\widetilde{a}))}

\displaystyle\quad+\left\|[(\nabla\Phi)^{-1}_{lw}(\nabla\Phi)^{-1}_{no}(\nabla\Phi)_{vm}-\delta_{lw}\delta_{no}\delta_{vm}](\widetilde{e}_{2}\circ\Phi)_{w}\varepsilon_{lmn}(\partial_{v}(\widetilde{e}_{2})_{o})(\Phi)\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}(\widetilde{a}))}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\tau_{q}^{-1}\mu_{q}\Gamma_{q}^{5})(\lambda_{q}\Gamma_{q})+(\tau^{-1}_{q}\mu_{q}\Gamma_{q}^{5})\cdot\frac{\lambda_{q*}^{2}}{\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}}\lesssim(\tau_{q}^{-1}\mu_{q}\Gamma_{q}^{5})\frac{\lambda_{q*}^{2}}{\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}}\,.

From this, (
5.26
), (
3.14
), (
2.30
),
and
[
29
, Lemma A.1]
, we can estimate
H_{11}
by

|H_{11}|\lesssim\frac{\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{2}}{\lambda_{q*}^{3}}\cdot(\tau_{q}^{-1}\mu_{q}\Gamma_{q}^{5})\frac{\lambda_{q*}^{2}}{\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}}\,\lesssim\frac{\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}}{\lambda_{q*}}(\tau_{q}^{-1}\mu_{q}\Gamma_{q}^{5})\underset{\eqref{eq:qho:choice2}}{\leq}\lambda_{q}\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}}(\tau_{q}^{-1}\mu_{q}\Gamma_{q}^{5})\underset{\eqref{eq:defn:tau}}{\leq}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\,.

(6.7)

Next, we may estimate
H_{13}
in a similar way as the high frequency part of
H_{12}
by integrating by parts many times. Specifically, we use that
(\varrho^{2}\circ\Psi)
is
(\mathbb{T}/\lambda_{q+\nicefrac{{{\bar{n}}}}{{2}}}\Gamma_{q})^{3}
-periodic
,
\widetilde{a}(\Phi^{-1})
has derivative cost
\lambda_{q+\nicefrac{{{\bar{n}}}}{{2}}}
from item
iv
in Lemma
5.7
, and the parameter condition (
8.12a
) to obtain that

\displaystyle|H_{13}|

\displaystyle=\left|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,k,\vec{l},I\end{subarray}}\frac{\lambda_{q*}^{3}}{\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\widetilde{a}^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{\neq 0})(\varrho^{2}\circ\Psi))(\Phi)dx\right|=\left|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,k,\vec{l},I\end{subarray}}\frac{\lambda_{q*}^{3}}{\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\widetilde{a}^{2}(\Phi^{-1})\mathbb{P}_{\neq 0}(\varrho^{2}\circ\Psi)dx\right|\leq\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{-1}

(6.8)

Step 5: Conclusion.
Combining (
6.7
), (
6.5
), (
6.6
), and (
6.8
), we obtain

\displaystyle h(t)-H(u_{q})-H(w_{q+1,h}^{(p)})=h(t)-H(u_{q})-H_{1}=(h(t)-H(u_{q}))\left(\frac{\Gamma_{q-1}}{2\Gamma_{q}}\right)+O(\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1})\,,

and using the inductive assumption (
2.30
), we obtain
\frac{\Gamma_{q-1}}{4\Gamma_{q}^{2}}\leq h(t)-H(u_{q})-H(w_{q+1,h}^{(p)})\leq\frac{3}{4}\Gamma_{q}^{-1}
.
∎

Lemma 6.2

(
Computing negligible Fourier modes
)
.

There exists a choice of
{\mathsf{D}}
from Proposition
4.5, []
and
3.3
so that the following holds.

(i)

For any
m\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}
satisfying
|m|\leq\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}\Gamma_{q-100}^{-\frac{1}{1000}}
,
\left|\mathcal{F}(w_{q+1})(m)\right|\leq{\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{-23}}
.

(ii)

For any
m\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}
satisfying
|m|\geq\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}\Gamma_{q}
,
\left|\mathcal{F}(w_{q+1})(m)\right|\leq{|m|^{-24}}
.

Proof.

We begin with the first claim. We will only estimate
|\mathcal{F}(w_{q+1,R}^{(p)})(m)|
. The estimates of
|\mathcal{F}(w_{q+1,R}^{(c)})(m)|
and
|\mathcal{F}(w_{q+1,h}^{(p)})(m)|
then follow in a similar way. Recalling that

\displaystyle w_{q+1,R}^{(p)}=\sum_{i,k,\xi,\vec{l},I}\underbrace{a_{(\xi)}(t,y)\left(\boldsymbol{\rho}_{(\xi)}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I}\right)\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1}}_{=:A}\mathbb{W}_{(\xi)}^{I}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\,,

we write

\displaystyle|\mathcal{F}(w_{q+1,R}^{(p)}(t))(m)|

\displaystyle=\left|\sum_{i,k,\xi,\vec{l},I}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}A\mathbb{W}_{(\xi)}^{I}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}e^{-2\pi im\cdot x}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\sum_{i,k,\xi,\vec{l},I}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\xi\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{-{\mathsf{D}}}\mathrm{div}^{\mathsf{D}}\left(\vartheta^{k}_{\xi,\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}},r_{q}}\right)A\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1}e^{-2\pi im\cdot\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1}}dx\right|

\displaystyle=\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{-{\mathsf{D}}}\left|\sum_{i,k,\xi,\vec{l},I}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\vartheta^{k}_{\xi,\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}},r_{q}}\right):\nabla^{\mathsf{D}}\left(A\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1}e^{-2\pi im\cdot\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1}}\right)dx\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda_{{q+{\bar{n}}}}^{-{\mathsf{D}}}\left(\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}\Gamma_{q-100}^{-\frac{1}{1000}}\right)^{{\mathsf{D}}}\delta_{{q+{\bar{n}}}}^{1/2}<\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{23}}\,.

The proof of the second estimate is even easier: integrating by parts
\mathsf{N}_{\rm ind}
times, we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{F}(w_{q+1}(t))(m)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}e^{-2\pi im\cdot x}w_{q+1}(t,x)\,dx\right|=\left|\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\Delta^{-\lceil\mathsf{N}_{\rm ind}/2\rceil}{e^{-2\pi im\cdot x}}\Delta^{\lceil\mathsf{N}_{\rm ind}/2\rceil}w_{q+1}(t,x)\,dx\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|2\pi m|^{24}(\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}\Gamma_{q})^{\mathsf{N}_{\rm ind}-24}}\|D^{\mathsf{N}_{\rm ind}}w_{q+1}(t,x)\|_{L^{1}}\lesssim\frac{1}{|m|^{24}(\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}\Gamma_{q})^{\mathsf{N}_{\rm ind}-24}}\lambda_{{q+{\bar{n}}}}^{\mathsf{N}_{\rm ind}}\delta_{{q+{\bar{n}}}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq|m|^{-24}\Gamma_{q}^{-\mathsf{N}_{\rm ind}+24}\lambda_{{q+{\bar{n}}}}^{24}\delta_{{q+{\bar{n}}}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\underset{\eqref{eq:Nind:3}}{<}\frac{1}{|m|^{24}}\,.

∎

Proposition 6.3

(
Frequency localization
)
.

Hypothesis
2.8
is true at step
q+1
.

Proof.

Let
\varphi_{i}
for
i=1,2
be as in Hypothesis
2.8
at step
q+1
. By the definition of
\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}
in (
5.33
),

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{P}_{\varphi_{i}}\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\|\mathbb{P}_{\varphi_{i}}\mathcal{\widetilde{P}}_{q+{\bar{n}},x,t}w_{q+1}\|_{\infty}\leq\|\mathcal{\widetilde{P}}_{q+{\bar{n}},x,t}\|_{L^{1}\to L^{1}}\|\mathbb{P}_{\varphi_{i}}w_{q+1}\|_{\infty}=\|\mathbb{P}_{\varphi_{i}}w_{q+1}\|_{\infty}\,.

Thus, it suffices to estimate
\|\mathbb{P}_{\varphi_{i}}w_{q+1}\|_{\infty}
. For
i=1
, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{P}_{\varphi_{1}}w_{q+1}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\left\|\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\varphi_{1}(m)\mathcal{F}(w_{q+1})(m)e^{2\pi im\cdot x}\right\|_{\infty}=\left\|\sum_{|m|\leq k_{1}}\varphi_{1}(m)\mathcal{F}(w_{q+1})(m)e^{2\pi im\cdot x}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{|m|\leq k_{1}}|\varphi_{1}(m)||\mathcal{F}(w_{q+1})(m)|\leq\left\|\varphi_{1}\right\|_{\infty}\sum_{|m|\leq k_{1}}\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{23}}\leq\frac{k_{1}}{\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{20}}\,.

Similarly, for
i=2
, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{P}_{\varphi_{2}}w_{q+1}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{|m|\geq k_{2}}|\varphi_{2}(m)||\mathcal{F}(w_{q+1})(m)|\leq\left\|\varphi_{2}\right\|_{\infty}\sum_{|m|\geq k_{2}}\frac{1}{|m|^{24}}\leq\frac{\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}}{k_{2}^{20}}\,.

∎

Before estimating the helicity from
w_{q+1,h}^{(c)}
and
w_{q+1,R}
, we introduce the following lemma.

Lemma 6.4

(
Commutators and dodging
)
.

Let
p\in[1,\infty]
and
\lambda
,
\Lambda
, and
\Gamma
be positive constants. Let
G:\mathbb{T}^{3}\to\mathbb{R}
and
H:\mathbb{T}^{3}\to\mathbb{R}
satisfy
\|G\|_{C^{1}}\leq\mathcal{C}_{G}\lambda
and
\|H\|_{L^{p}}\leq\mathcal{C}_{H}
.
Then, for any multiplier
\varphi:\mathbb{R}^{3}\rightarrow B
such that

\|\check{\varphi}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leq 1\,,\qquad\|\mathbf{1}_{F}\check{\varphi}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leq\Lambda^{-100}\,,\qquad\textnormal{ where }F=\left(B_{{(\Lambda\Gamma^{-2})^{-1}}}\right)^{c}\,,

(6.9)

we have the following.

(i)

The commutator
[\mathbb{P}_{\varphi},G]H:=\mathbb{P}_{\varphi}(GH)-G\mathbb{P}_{\varphi}H
satisfies

\|[\mathbb{P}_{\varphi},G]H\|_{L^{p}}\lesssim\lambda\Gamma^{2}\Lambda^{-1}\mathcal{C}_{G}\mathcal{C}_{H}\,.

(6.10)

(ii)

The multiplier operator can be decomposed into
\mathbb{P}_{\varphi}=\mathbb{P}_{\varphi}^{\text{loc}}+\mathbb{P}_{\varphi}^{\text{nl}}
such that the local part satisfies

\mathrm{supp\,}(\mathbb{P}_{\varphi}^{\text{loc}}(GH))\subset B(\mathrm{supp\,}(GH),\Lambda^{-1}\Gamma^{2})\,

(6.11)

and the nonlocal part satisfies

\|\mathbb{P}_{\varphi}^{\text{nl}}(GH)\|_{L^{p}}\lesssim\lambda\Lambda^{-100}\mathcal{C}_{G}\mathcal{C}_{H}\,.

(6.12)

Proof.

Denote by
\varphi^{loc}
the multiplier with kernel
\check{\varphi}^{loc}=\mathbf{1}_{B_{\Lambda^{-1}\Gamma^{2}}}\check{\varphi}
and by
\varphi^{nl}
the multiplier with kernel
\check{\varphi}^{nl}=\mathbf{1}_{F}\check{\varphi}
. Then
\varphi=\varphi^{loc}+\varphi^{nl}
. Now we define
\mathbb{P}_{\varphi}^{loc}:=\mathbb{P}_{\varphi^{loc}}
and
\mathbb{P}_{\varphi}^{nl}:=\mathbb{P}_{\varphi^{nl}}
. Note that
[\mathbb{P}_{\varphi},G]=[\mathbb{P}_{\varphi^{loc}},G]+[\mathbb{P}_{\varphi^{nl}},G]
. Since
\mathrm{supp\,}(\check{\varphi}^{loc})\subset B_{\Lambda^{-1}\Gamma^{2}}
, we have (
6.11
). We estimate the local part of the commutator:

\displaystyle\|[\mathbb{P}_{\varphi^{loc}},G]H\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle=\left\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\check{\varphi}^{loc}(y)\left(G(x-y)-G(x)\right)H(x-y)\,dy\right\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left|\check{\varphi}^{loc}(y)\left(G(x-y)-G(x)\right)H(x-y)\right|\,dy\right\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leq\|\nabla G\|_{L^{\infty}}\left\|\int_{B_{\Lambda^{-1}\Gamma^{2}}}|\check{\varphi}(y)||y||H(x-y)|\,dy\right\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\nabla G\|_{L^{\infty}}\Lambda^{-1}\Gamma^{2}\left\|\int|\check{\varphi}(y)||H(x-y)|\,dy\right\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda\Gamma^{2}{\Lambda}^{-1}\mathcal{C}_{G}\mathcal{C}_{H}\,.

The estimate of the nonlocal part (
6.12
), on the other hand, can be obtained as follows:

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{P}_{\varphi^{nl}}(GH)\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle=\left\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\check{\varphi}^{nl}(y)(GH)(x-y)\,dy\right\|_{L^{p}}\lesssim\|\check{\varphi}^{nl}\|_{L^{1}}\|GH\|_{L^{p}}\leq\lambda\Lambda^{-100}\mathcal{C}_{G}\mathcal{C}_{H}\,.

Similarly,

\displaystyle\|G\mathbb{P}_{\varphi^{nl}}H\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\|G\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\mathbb{P}_{\varphi^{nl}}H\|_{L^{p}}\lesssim\mathcal{C}_{G}\|\check{\varphi}^{nl}\|_{L^{1}}\|H\|_{L^{p}}\leq\Lambda^{-100}\mathcal{C}_{G}\lambda\|H\|_{L^{p}}\,.

Putting these estimates together yields (
6.10
).
∎

Lemma 6.5

(
Computing negligible contributions to helicity
)
.

Let
\varphi:\mathbb{R}^{3}\rightarrow B
be any multiplier such that
6
6

6

In a slight abuse of notation, we allow
\varphi\equiv 1
, so that
\check{\varphi}=\delta_{0}
is the Dirac delta.

\|\check{\varphi}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leq 1\,,\qquad\|\mathbf{1}_{F_{q+1}}\check{\varphi}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leq\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{-100}\,,\qquad\textnormal{ where }F_{q+1}=\left(B_{(\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{-2})^{-1}}\right)^{c}\,.

(6.13)

Then the new perturbation
w_{q+1}=w_{q+1}^{(p)}+w_{q+1}^{(c)}+w_{q+1,h}^{(p)}+w^{(c)}_{q+1,h}
satisfies

\left|\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathbb{P}_{\varphi}(w_{q+1})\cdot\mathrm{curl\,}w_{q+1}-\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathbb{P}_{\varphi}(w_{q+1,h}^{(p)})\cdot\mathrm{curl\,}w_{q+1,h}^{(p)}\right|\lesssim\Gamma_{q+1}^{-2}\,.

(6.14)

In addition, for all
M\leq\nicefrac{{\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{3}}
,

\left|\left[\nicefrac{{d}}{{dt}}\right]^{M}\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}(w_{q+1})\cdot\mathrm{curl\,}w_{q+1}-\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}(w_{q+1,h}^{(p)})\cdot\mathrm{curl\,}w_{q+1,h}^{(p)}\right)\right|\lesssim\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\mu_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{20},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{8}\right)\,.

(6.15)

Proof.

Using (
5.32
) and
the dodging properties from Lemma
5.11
, specifically (
5.41
), we have that

\displaystyle H(\mathbb{P}_{\varphi}w_{q+1},w_{q+1})-H(\mathbb{P}_{\varphi}w_{q+1,h}^{(p)},w_{q+1,h}^{(p)})

(6.16)

\displaystyle=H(\mathbb{P}_{\varphi}w_{q+1,R}^{(p)},w_{q+1,R}^{(p)})+2H(\mathbb{P}_{\varphi}w_{q+1,R}^{(p)},w_{q+1,R}^{(c)})+H(\mathbb{P}_{\varphi}w_{q+1,R}^{(c)},w_{q+1,R}^{(c)})

\displaystyle\quad+2H(\mathbb{P}_{\varphi}w_{q+1,h}^{(p)},w_{q+1,h}^{(c)})+H(\mathbb{P}_{\varphi}w_{q+1,h}^{(c)},w_{q+1,h}^{(c)})\,.

(6.17)

We therefore estimate each term on the right-hand side of (
6.17
).

Case 1: Euler-Reynolds principal-principal part
H(\mathbb{P}_{\varphi}w_{q+1,R}^{(p)},w_{q+1,R}^{(p)})
.

Step 1a: Commutator and off-diagonal terms.
By using the dodging properties between
\{w_{(\xi)}^{(p),I}\}
from
5.41
, and denoting
A=((\nabla{\Phi}_{(i,k)})^{-1})
, we compute the mollified helicity of
w_{q+1,R}^{(p)}

\displaystyle H\left(\mathbb{P}_{\varphi}\left(w_{q+1,R}^{(p)}\right),w_{q+1,R}^{(p)}\right)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathbb{P}_{\varphi}\left(w_{q+1,R}^{(p)}\right)\cdot\mathrm{curl\,}w_{q+1,R}^{(p)}dx

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I\end{subarray}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathbb{P}_{\varphi}\left(w_{(\xi)}^{(p),I}\right)\cdot\mathrm{curl\,}w_{(\xi)}^{(p),I}dx}_{\text{(diagonal)}}+\underbrace{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I\end{subarray}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathbb{P}_{\varphi}\left(w_{q+1,R}^{(p)}-w_{(\xi)}^{(p),I}\right)\cdot\mathrm{curl\,}w_{(\xi)}^{(p),I}dx}_{\text{(off-diagonal)}}\,.

We first deal with the off-diagonal term and show that it is negligibly small. Indeed, by the dodging property (
5.41
), we have that

\mathrm{supp\,}\left(w_{q+1,R}^{(p)}-w_{(\xi)}^{(p),I}\right)\cap B\left(\mathrm{supp\,}(w_{(\xi)}^{(p),I}),\frac{1}{4}\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{2}\right)=\emptyset\,.

Then, Lemma
6.4
implies that

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{P}_{\varphi}\left(w_{q+1,R}^{(p)}-w_{(\xi)}^{(p),I}\right)

\displaystyle\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}w_{(\xi)}^{(p),I})}\leq\|\mathbb{P}_{\varphi^{nl}}\left(w_{q+1,R}^{(p)}-w_{(\xi)}^{(p),I}\right)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbf{1}_{F_{q+1}}\check{\varphi}\|_{L^{1}}\|w_{q+1,R}^{(p)}-w_{(\xi)}^{(p),I}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{-100}\Gamma_{q}^{\frac{\mathsf{C_{\infty}}}{2}+{16}}r_{q}^{-1}\,.

Thus, we obtain

\displaystyle|\text{(off-diagonal)}|\leq\|\mathbb{P}_{\varphi}\left(w_{q+1,R}^{(p)}-w_{(\xi)}^{(p),I}\right)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}w_{(\xi)}^{(p),I})}\|\mathrm{curl\,}w_{(\xi)}^{(p),I}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{-99}\Gamma_{q}^{\frac{\mathsf{C_{\infty}}}{2}+{20}}\underset{\eqref{def:thursday}}{\leq}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-2}

which is negligibly small. We move on to estimate the diagonal term:

(diagonal)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I\end{subarray}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathbb{P}_{\varphi}\left(a_{(\xi)}\left(\boldsymbol{\rho}_{(\xi)}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I}\right)\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}(\nabla{\Phi}_{(i,k)})^{-1}\xi\varrho_{(\xi)}^{I}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\right)\cdot\mathrm{curl\,}w_{(\xi)}^{(p),I}dx

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I\end{subarray}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}A\xi\mathbb{P}_{\varphi}\left(a_{(\xi)}\left(\boldsymbol{\rho}_{(\xi)}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I}\right)\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\varrho_{(\xi)}^{I}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\right)\cdot\mathrm{curl\,}w_{(\xi)}^{(p),I}dx}_{\text{(main)}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\underbrace{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I\end{subarray}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}[\mathbb{P}_{\varphi},A\xi]\left(a_{(\xi)}\left(\boldsymbol{\rho}_{(\xi)}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I}\right)\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\varrho_{(\xi)}^{I}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\right)\cdot\mathrm{curl\,}w_{(\xi)}^{(p),I}dx}_{\text{(commutator)}}

We now estimate the commutator term above using Lemma
6.4
with the following choices:

\displaystyle p=2,\,

\displaystyle\varphi=\varphi,\,\Lambda=\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}},\,F=F_{q+1},\,G=(\nabla{\Phi}_{(i,k)})^{-1}\xi,\,\mathcal{C}_{G}=\tau_{q}^{-1}\mu_{q},\,\lambda=\lambda_{q},\,\Gamma=\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}}

\displaystyle H=a_{(\xi)}\left(\boldsymbol{\rho}_{(\xi)}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I}\right)\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\varrho_{(\xi)}^{I}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)},\,\mathcal{C}_{H}=\left|\mathrm{supp\,}\left(\eta_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l}}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I}\right)\right|^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Gamma_{q}^{j+{10}}\,.

We then obtain from (
6.10
) that

\displaystyle\left\|[\mathbb{P}_{\varphi},A\xi]\left(a_{(\xi)}\left(\boldsymbol{\rho}_{(\xi)}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I}\right)\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\varrho_{(\xi)}^{I}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq\frac{\tau_{q}^{-1}\mu_{q}\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}}\left|\mathrm{supp\,}\left(\eta_{{(\xi)}}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I}\right)\right|^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Gamma_{q}^{j+{10}}

Also, from (
5.44a
), we get that
\left\|\mathrm{curl\,}w_{(\xi)}^{(p),I}\right\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\left|\mathrm{supp\,}\left(\eta_{{(\xi)}}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I}\right)\right|^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Gamma_{q}^{j+{10}}\lambda_{{q+{\bar{n}}}}
. Note that
\mathrm{curl\,}w_{(\xi)}^{(p),I}
is supported on
\mathrm{supp\,}\left(\eta_{{(\xi)}}\,\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\right)
and so, by Corollary
5.9
with
p=1,\theta_{1}=0,\theta_{2}=2
,

\displaystyle|\text{(commutator)}|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I\end{subarray}}\left\|[\mathbb{P}_{\varphi},A\xi]\left(a_{(\xi)}\left(\boldsymbol{\rho}_{(\xi)}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I}\right)\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\varrho_{(\xi)}^{I}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\mathrm{curl\,}w_{(\xi)}^{(p),I}\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\tau_{q}^{-1}\mu_{q}\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}}\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}}\lambda_{{q+{\bar{n}}}}\Gamma_{q}^{26}\underset{\eqref{eq:defn:tau}}{\leq}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-4}

Step 1b: Main term and time derivatives.
Now we estimate the main term with mollifiers but no time derivatives. Writing
B^{I}_{(\xi)}=a_{(\xi)}\left(\boldsymbol{\rho}_{(\xi)}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I}\right)\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\varrho_{(\xi)}^{I}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}
and suppressing all indices in the summand, we decompose based on whether curl operator falls on
A\xi
or not:

(main)

\displaystyle={\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I\end{subarray}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}A\xi(\mathbb{P}_{\varphi}B)B\cdot\mathrm{curl\,}(A\xi)dx}+{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I\end{subarray}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}A\xi(\mathbb{P}_{\varphi}B)\cdot\left((\nabla B)\times(A\xi)\right)dx}\,.

(6.18)

The second term vanishes due to the fact that
(\nabla B)\times(A\xi)
is orthogonal to
A\xi
.

So it remains to estimate the first term.
Since
\xi
is constant,
\xi\cdot\mathrm{curl\,}\xi=0
and (
5.4b
) gives

\left|A\xi\cdot\mathrm{curl\,}(A\xi)\right|=\left|A\xi\cdot\mathrm{curl\,}(A\xi)-\xi\cdot\mathrm{curl\,}\xi\right|\lesssim\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{-5}\mu_{q}\,.

Combining this with Corollary
5.9
with
\theta_{1}=0
and
\theta_{2}=2
, we estimate the first term by

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j,k,\vec{l},\xi,I\end{subarray}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}(\mathbb{P}_{\varphi}B)B\left(A\xi\cdot\mathrm{curl\,}(A\xi)-\xi\cdot\mathrm{curl\,}\xi\right)\,dx\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\tau_{q}^{-1}\mu_{q}\Gamma_{q}^{5}(\lambda_{q}\Gamma_{q})\cdot\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}}\underset{\eqref{eq:defn:tau}}{\leq}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-2}

(6.19)

Finally, we estimate time derivatives of
H(w_{q+1,R}^{(p)})
. Since there is no mollifier present, we need only consider the analogue of (
6.18
), but with time derivatives and no mollifiers:

\left|\left[\nicefrac{{d}}{{dt}}\right]^{M}{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I\end{subarray}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}A\xi B^{2}\cdot\mathrm{curl\,}(A\xi)dx}+\left[\nicefrac{{d}}{{dt}}\right]^{M}{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I\end{subarray}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}A\xi B\cdot\left((\nabla B)\times(A\xi)\right)dx}\,\right|.

The second term vanishes again, and we add and subtract
\xi\cdot\mathrm{curl\,}\xi
in the first term again. Then converting time derivatives outside the integral to material derivatives inside the integral and using Corollary
5.4
, Lemma
5.13
, and Corollary
5.9
as above, we obtain an estimate analogous to (
6.19
), but multiplied by the cost of the time derivatives, consistent with (
6.15
).

Case 2: Helical corrector parts
2H(\mathbb{P}_{\varphi}w_{q+1,h}^{(p)},w_{q+1,h}^{(c)})+H(\mathbb{P}_{\varphi}w_{q+1,h}^{(c)})
.

The estimates are made in the same fashion as
Step 1
of the proof of Lemma
6.1
, in which we proved (
6.2
) and (
6.3
); notice these estimates required no orthogonality properties and were made based purely on amplitude. However, the estimates in this case are even better, since the helicity corrector
w_{q+1,h}^{(c)}
obeys a better bound than
w_{q+1,h}^{(p)}
from Corollary
5.14
. We omit further details.

Case 3: Euler-Reynolds principal-corrector part
2H(\mathbb{P}_{\varphi}w_{q+1,R}^{(p)},w_{q+1,R}^{(c)})
.
As in
Case 1, Step 1a
, we first decompose into a diagonal and off-diagonal part:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathbb{P}_{\varphi}w_{q+1,R}^{(p)}\cdot\mathrm{curl\,}w_{q+1,R}^{(c)}dx

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\sum_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathbb{P}_{\varphi}w_{q+1,(\xi)}^{(p),I}\cdot\mathrm{curl\,}w_{q+1,(\xi)}^{(c),I}\,dx}_{\text{(diagonal)}}+\underbrace{\sum_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathbb{P}_{\varphi}\left(w_{q+1,R}^{(p)}-w_{q+1,(\xi)}^{(p),I}\right)\cdot\mathrm{curl\,}w_{q+1,(\xi)}^{(c),I}\,dx}_{\text{(off-diagonal)}}\,.

The off-diagonal term is negligibly small by the same argument as before. We move on to the diagonal term. We set
B=B_{(\xi)}^{I}=a_{(\xi)}\left(\boldsymbol{\rho}_{(\xi)}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I}\right)\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}
, drop indices for concision, and write

(diagonal)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathbb{P}_{\varphi}w_{q+1,(\xi)}^{(p),I}\varepsilon_{mln}\varepsilon_{nop}\partial_{l}\left(\partial_{o}B\partial_{p}\Phi^{r}{\mathbb{U}}_{r}(\Phi)\right)dx

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\sum_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathbb{P}_{\varphi}w_{q+1,(\xi)}^{(p),I}\varepsilon_{mln}\varepsilon_{nop}\partial_{l}\left(\partial_{o}B\partial_{p}\Phi^{r}\right){\mathbb{U}}_{r}(\Phi)dx}_{I_{1}}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\sum_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathbb{P}_{\varphi}w_{q+1,(\xi)}^{(p),I}\varepsilon_{mln}\varepsilon_{nop}\partial_{o}B\partial_{p}\Phi^{r}\partial_{l}\left({\mathbb{U}}_{r}(\Phi)\right)dx}_{I_{2}}\,.

(6.20)

We estimate
I_{1}
directly. Using aggregation lemmas as usual, (
5.42b
), and (
3.5
),

\displaystyle|I_{1}|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left|\mathbb{P}_{\varphi}w_{q+1,(\xi)}^{(p),I}\varepsilon_{mln}\varepsilon_{nop}\partial_{l}\left(\partial_{o}B\partial_{p}\Phi^{r}\right){\mathbb{U}}_{r}(\Phi)\right|dx

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I}\left\|\mathbb{P}_{\varphi}w_{q+1,(\xi)}^{(p),I}\right\|_{L^{2}}\|\varepsilon_{mln}\varepsilon_{nop}\partial_{l}\left(\partial_{o}B\partial_{p}\Phi^{r}\right){\mathbb{U}}_{r}(\Phi)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I}\left\|w_{q+1,(\xi)}^{(p),I}\right\|_{L^{2}}\|D\left(DB(\nabla\Phi)^{T}\right)\|_{2}\|{\mathbb{U}}(\Phi)\|_{2}\lesssim\frac{\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}/2}^{2}}{\lambda_{{q+{\bar{n}}}}}\delta_{{q+{\bar{n}}}}\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{500}\underset{\eqref{par:helcc}}{\lesssim}\Gamma_{{q+{\bar{n}}}}^{-100}\,.

(6.21)

We move now to
I_{2}
; recalling that
A=(\nabla\Phi)^{-1}
, we write this term as

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\sum_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}A\xi\,\mathbb{P}_{\varphi}\left(B\,\varrho(\Phi)\right)\varepsilon_{mln}\varepsilon_{nop}\partial_{o}B\partial_{p}\Phi^{r}\partial_{l}\left({\mathbb{U}}_{r}(\Phi)\right)dx}_{\text{(main)}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\underbrace{\sum_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left[\mathbb{P}_{\varphi},A\xi\right]\left(A\,\varrho(\Phi)\right)\varepsilon_{mln}\varepsilon_{nop}\partial_{o}B\partial_{p}\Phi^{r}\partial_{l}\left({\mathbb{U}}_{r}(\Phi)\right)dx}_{\text{(commutator)}}

The commutator term can be estimated similarly as in
Case 1, Step 1a
, yielding

|\text{(commutator)}|\lesssim\frac{\mu_{q}\tau_{q}^{-1}\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}}\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}}\lambda_{q+\nicefrac{{{\bar{n}}}}{{2}}}\Gamma_{q}^{26}\leq\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{-4}\,.

Next, using
\varepsilon_{mln}\varepsilon_{nop}=\delta_{mo}\delta_{lp}-\delta_{mp}\delta_{lo}
, the main term from
I_{2}
can be rewritten as

\displaystyle|\text{(main)}|

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I}\int\left(A\xi\right)_{m}\mathbb{P}_{\varphi}\left(B\,\varrho(\Phi)\right)\left(\partial_{m}B\partial_{l}\Phi^{r}-\partial_{l}B\partial_{m}\Phi^{r}\right)\partial_{l}\Phi^{s}(\partial_{s}{\mathbb{U}}_{r})(\Phi)dx\,.

(6.22)

We note that the term with
\partial_{m}\Phi^{r}
above vanishes due to orthogonality; indeed as
{\mathbb{U}}\cdot\vec{\xi}=0
,
\partial_{s}{\mathbb{U}}
remains orthogonal to
\vec{e}_{2}
for any
s
, and so using
A_{j}^{m}\partial_{m}\Phi^{r}=\delta_{jr}
,

(A\xi)_{m}\partial_{m}\Phi^{r}(\partial_{s}\mathbb{U}_{r})(\Phi)=A_{j}^{m}\xi^{j}\partial_{m}\Phi^{r}(\partial_{s}{\mathbb{U}}_{r})(\Phi)=\xi_{r}(\partial_{s}{\mathbb{U}}_{r})(\Phi)=0\,.

So we must estimate the term with
\partial_{m}B\partial_{l}\Phi^{r}
. We recall from (
5.42b
) that

\left\|\left(A\xi\right)_{m}\partial_{m}B\right\|_{2}\lesssim\left|\mathrm{supp\,}\eta_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l}}\right|^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Gamma^{j+10}_{q}{\Gamma_{q}^{5}\Lambda_{q}}\,.

Next, we have from Corollary
5.4
and (
3.5
) that

\|D_{t,q}\left(\partial_{{l}}\Phi^{r}\partial_{l}\Phi^{s}(\partial_{s}{\mathbb{U}}_{r})(\Phi)\right)\|_{2}=\|D_{t,q}\left(\partial_{{l}}\Phi^{r}\partial_{l}\Phi^{s}\right)(\partial_{s}{\mathbb{U}}_{r})(\Phi)\|_{2}\lesssim\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{-5}

and that when
\Phi\equiv\mathrm{Id}
at
t=k\mu_{q}\Gamma_{q}^{-i-2}
,
\partial_{{l}}\Phi^{r}\partial_{l}\Phi_{s}(\partial_{s}{\mathbb{U}}_{r})(\Phi)=\partial_{l}\mathbb{U}_{l}\equiv 0
. Thus, by transport estimates on the
\mu_{q}
timescale, we have that
\|\partial_{l}\Phi^{r}\partial_{l}\Phi^{s}(\partial_{s}{\mathbb{U}}_{r})(\Phi)\|_{2}\lesssim\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{-5}\mu_{q}
. Using the above bounds and aggregation estimates as usual, we conclude that

\displaystyle|I_{2}|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I}\left\|\left(A\xi\right)_{m}\partial_{m}B\partial_{l}\Phi^{r}\partial_{l}\Phi_{s}(\partial_{s}{\mathbb{U}}_{r})(\Phi)\right\|_{2}\left\|\mathbb{P}_{\varphi}\left(B\,\varrho(\Phi)\right)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I}\left\|\left(A\xi\right)_{m}\partial_{m}B\right\|_{2}\left\|\partial_{l}\Phi^{r}\partial_{l}\Phi_{s}(\partial_{s}{\mathbb{U}}_{r})(\Phi)\right\|_{2}\left\|B\,\varrho(\Phi)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q}^{3}\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Gamma_{q}^{15}\Lambda_{q}\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{-5}\mu_{q}\delta_{{q+{\bar{n}}}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\underset{\eqref{eq:defn:tau}}{\lesssim}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-2}\,.

Finally, we must estimate time derivatives of
H(w_{q+1,R}^{(p)},w_{q+1,R}^{(c)})
. As there is no mollifier present, we need only take time derivatives of the diagonal terms
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
where
\varphi\equiv 1
. Estimating
I_{1}
but incorporating time derivatives (which are converted to material derivatives inside the integral as usual), we get an estimate analogous to (
6.21
), but multiplied the cost of material derivatives, consistent with (
6.15
). Next,
I_{2}
has no commutator term, and the second term from (
6.22
) still vanishes by orthogonality. Converting the time derivatives outside the integral in the first to to material derivatives inside the integral, but retracing the above estimates with extra material derivatives, we obtain a bound consistent with (
6.15
).

Case 4: Euler-Reynolds corrector-corrector part
H(\mathbb{P}_{\varphi}w_{q+1,R}^{(c)},w_{q+1,R}^{(c)})
.

A heuristic estimate gives

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathrm{curl\,}w_{q+1,R}^{(c)}\cdot w_{q+1,R}^{(c)}dx\right|\lesssim\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}}\frac{\lambda_{q+\frac{{\bar{n}}}{2}}^{2}}{\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{2}}\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}\Gamma_{q}^{10}\underset{\eqref{par:helcc}}{\leq}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-2}\,.

Using aggregation lemmas and taking time derivatives as in the previous steps, the above estimate can be made rigorous and is consistent with (
6.14
) and (
6.15
).
∎

Proposition 6.6

(
Verifying Hypothesis
2.7
)
.

Hypothesis
2.7
is true at step
q+1
.

Proof.

Let
\varphi
be as in the hypothesis. Note that from (
6.2
) and (
6.14
), we immediately have that

\left|\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathbb{P}_{\varphi}(w_{q+1})\cdot\mathrm{curl\,}w_{q+1}\right|<\frac{1}{5}\Gamma_{q-4}^{-1/2}\,.

Since
w_{q+1}-\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}
is negligibly small from (
5.47
), Hypothesis
2.7
then follows from direct computation; we omit further details.
∎

Lemma 6.7

(
Helicity interaction between
\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}
and
u_{q}
)
.

For
M\leq\nicefrac{{\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{3}}
,

\displaystyle\left|\left[\nicefrac{{d}}{{dt}}\right]^{M}H(u_{q},\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}})\right|\leq\Gamma_{q+1}^{-10}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\mu_{q}^{-1},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{8}\right)\,.

(6.23)

Proof.

We first claim that we have
|\left[\nicefrac{{d}}{{dt}}\right]^{M}H(u_{q},w_{q+1})|\leq\Gamma_{q+1}^{-15}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\mu_{q}^{-1},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{8}\right)
. To prove this, we only estimate
H(u_{q},w_{q+1,R}^{(p)})
. The estimates of
H(u_{q},w_{q+1,R}^{(c)})
and
H(u_{q},w_{q+1,h})
then follow similarly, because
w_{q+1,R}^{(c)}
and
w_{q+1,h}
have the same structure as
w_{q+1,R}^{(p)}
. Recalling that

\displaystyle w_{q+1,R}^{(p)}=\sum_{i,k,\xi,\vec{l},I}\underbrace{a_{(\xi)}(t,y)\left(\boldsymbol{\rho}_{(\xi)}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I}\right)\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1}}_{=:\widetilde{a}}\mathbb{W}_{(\xi)}^{I}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\,,

and using that
\widehat{u}_{q}
is divergence-free (so that
\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}d/dt(\cdot)=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}D_{t,q}(\cdot)
), we write

\displaystyle\left|\left[\nicefrac{{d}}{{dt}}\right]^{M}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}w_{q+1}^{(p)}\cdot\mathrm{curl\,}u_{q}\,dx\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\sum_{0\leq M^{\prime}\leq M}c_{M,M^{\prime}}\sum_{i,k,\xi,\vec{l},I}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left(D_{t,q}^{M^{\prime}}\widetilde{a}\right)\mathbb{W}_{(\xi)}^{I}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\cdot D_{t,q}^{M-M^{\prime}}\left(\mathrm{curl\,}u_{q}\right)\,dx\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\sum_{0\leq M^{\prime}\leq M}c_{M,M^{\prime}}\sum_{i,k,\xi,\vec{l},I}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\xi\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{-{\mathsf{d}}}\mathrm{div}^{\mathsf{d}}\left(\vartheta^{k}_{\xi,\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}},r_{q}}\right)\cdot\left(D_{t,q}^{M^{\prime}}\widetilde{a}\,D_{t,q}^{M-M^{\prime}}\mathrm{curl\,}u_{q}\right)\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1}dx\right|

\displaystyle=\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{-{\mathsf{d}}}\left|\sum_{0\leq M^{\prime}\leq M}c_{M,M^{\prime}}\sum_{i,k,\xi,\vec{l},I}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\vartheta^{k}_{\xi,\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}},r_{q}}\right):\nabla^{\mathsf{d}}\left(\left(D_{t,q}^{M^{\prime}}\widetilde{a}\xi\cdot D_{t,q}^{M-M^{\prime}}\mathrm{curl\,}u_{q}\right)\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1}\right)dx\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}-1}\left(\frac{\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}-1}}{\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}}\right)^{{\mathsf{d}}}\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\mu_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{20},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{8}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\Gamma_{q+1}^{-20}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\mu_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{20},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{8}\right)\,.

Note in the last inequality, we have used a large choice of
{\mathsf{d}}
in (
xvi
). Thus we obtain the claim.

Finally, the estimate for
\left[\nicefrac{{d}}{{dt}}\right]^{M}H(u_{q},\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}})
follows from (
5.47
).
∎

Proposition 6.8

(
Verifying Hypothesis
2.6
)
.

Hypothesis
2.6
is true at step
q+1
.

Proof.

Let
\varphi
be as in the hypothesis at step
q+1
. Note that it suffices to prove that

\displaystyle 2\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\leq h(t)-H(u_{q+1})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{15}{16}\Gamma_{q}^{-1}\,,

(6.24)

\displaystyle\left|H(u_{q+1})-\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathbb{P}_{\varphi}(u_{q+1})\cdot\mathrm{curl\,}u_{q+1}\right|

\displaystyle<\Gamma_{q}^{-2}\,.

(6.25)

To prove (
6.24
), we see from (
6.1
), (
6.14
) with
\varphi\equiv 1
, and (
5.47
) that

\frac{\Gamma_{q-1}}{2\Gamma_{q}^{2}}\leq h(t)-H(u_{q})-H(\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}})\leq\frac{7}{8}\Gamma_{q}^{-1}\,.

Since
H(u_{q+1})=H(u_{q})+H(\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}})+2H(u_{q},\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}})
, (
6.24
) follows from this and Lemma
6.7
.

It remains to prove (
6.25
). Notice that

H(u_{q+1})-\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathbb{P}_{\varphi}(u_{q+1})\cdot\mathrm{curl\,}u_{q+1}=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathbb{P}_{1-\varphi}(u_{q+1})\cdot\mathrm{curl\,}u_{q+1}

and that the multiplier
\widetilde{\varphi}:=(1-\varphi)/2
satisfies
\widetilde{\varphi}:\mathbb{Z}^{3}\to B
and
\widetilde{\varphi}(m)=0
for all
|m|\leq\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}}
. Therefore, by Hypothesis
2.8
proved at step
q+1
in Proposition
6.3
, we have that

\|\mathbb{P}_{\widetilde{\varphi}}u_{q+1}\|_{\infty}=\sum_{i\leq q+1}\left\|\mathbb{P}_{\widetilde{\varphi}}\widehat{w}_{i+{\bar{n}}-1}\right\|_{\infty}\leq(q+{\bar{n}})\frac{\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}}{(\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}})^{20}}<\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{-19}\,.

Thus we have

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathbb{P}_{1-\varphi}(u_{q+1})\cdot\mathrm{curl\,}u_{q+1}\right|\leq\|\mathbb{P}_{1-\varphi}(u_{q+1})\|_{\infty}\|\mathrm{curl\,}u_{q+1}\|_{1}\leq\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{-10}<\Gamma_{q}^{-2}\,.

∎

Lemma 6.9

(
Verifying Hypothesis
2.9
)
.

H(u_{q+1})
satisfies

\left\|\left[\nicefrac{{d}}{{dt}}\right]^{M}H(u_{q+1})\right\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T])}\lesssim\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\mu_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{20},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{8}\right)\,.

(6.26)

Proof.

We split
H(u_{q+1})=H(u_{q})+2H(u_{q},\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}})+H(w_{q+1})+H(\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}})-H(w_{q+1})
. Applying (
2.34
) to the first term, (
6.23
) to the second term, (
6.3
) and (
6.15
) to the third term, and (
5.47
) to the final term yields the result.
∎

6.2 Energy increment

In this subsection, we verify (
2.28
) for
u_{q+1}=u_{q}+\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}
.
We first write

\displaystyle e(t)-\frac{1}{2}\|u_{q+1}(t,\cdot)\|_{2}^{2}=e(t)-\frac{1}{2}\left\|u_{q}\right\|_{2}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\|w_{q+1,R}^{(p)}\|_{2}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\int|\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}|^{2}-|w_{q+1,R}^{(p)}|^{2}-\int u_{q}\cdot\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}.

By the construction of
w_{q+1,R}^{(p)}
(see (
5.19
)), recalling that
R_{\ell}
is trace-free (see (
4.1
)), and following the computation as in
[
29
, Section 8.2]

we have

\displaystyle\int|w_{q+1,R}^{(p)}|^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I}\int a_{(\xi)}^{2}\left(\boldsymbol{\rho}_{(\xi)}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I}\right)^{2}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}|\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1}\xi|^{2}\left(\varrho_{(\xi),R}^{I}\right)^{2}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}

\displaystyle=2e(t)-\|u_{q}\|_{2}^{2}-\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}+1}+\sum_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l}}\int a_{(\xi)}^{2}\left(\mathbb{P}_{\neq 0}\boldsymbol{\rho}_{(\xi)}^{2}\right)\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}|\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1}\xi|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I}\int a_{(\xi)}^{2}\left(\boldsymbol{\rho}_{(\xi)}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I}\right)^{2}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}|\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1}\xi|^{2}\left(\mathbb{P}_{\neq 0}\left(\varrho_{(\xi),R}^{I}\right)^{2}\right)\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}

\displaystyle=2e(t)-\|u_{q}\|_{2}^{2}-\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}+1}+I_{1}+I_{2}\,.

To estimate
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
, we integrate by parts many times to bound these terms by
\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}+1}/50
. Thus

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}+1}-\frac{1}{50}\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}+1}\leq e(t)-\frac{1}{2}\left\|u_{q}\right\|_{2}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\|w_{q+1,R}^{(p)}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}+1}+\frac{1}{50}\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}+1}

Then, using the estimates in Corollary
5.14
and Lemma
5.15
together with Corollary
5.9
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left|\int|\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}|^{2}-|w_{q+1,R}^{(p)}|^{2}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\left(\|\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}-w_{q+1}\|_{2}+\|w_{q+1,R}^{(c)}\|_{2}+\left\|w_{q+1,h}\right\|_{2}\right)\left(\|\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}\|_{2}+\|w_{q+1,R}^{(p)}\|_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Gamma_{q}^{50}\left(\frac{\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}}{\lambda_{q*}^{\frac{3}{2}}}+\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{\frac{1}{2}}r_{q}\right)\leq\frac{1}{50}\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}+1}\,.

Arguing as in Lemma
6.7
and using (
5.47
),

\left|\int u_{q}\cdot\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}\right|\leq\frac{1}{50}\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}+1}
. Combining gives (
2.28
).

7
Error estimates

7.1 Identification of error terms

We define the new Reynolds stress
S_{q+1}
by adding
\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}
to the Euler-Reynolds system for
(u_{q},p_{q},R_{q})
in Lemma
4.1
. We use (
5.41
) in the string of equalities below, which prevents
w_{q+1,R}
and
w_{q+1,h}
from interacting nonlinearly to produce an oscillation error.

\displaystyle\mathrm{div}(S_{q+1})

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}+(u_{q}\cdot\nabla)\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}+(\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}\cdot\nabla)u_{q}

\displaystyle\quad+\mathrm{div}(\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}\otimes\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}-P_{q+1}\mathrm{Id}+R_{\ell})+\mathrm{div}\left(R_{q}^{q}-\frac{1}{3}(\mathrm{tr\,}R_{q}^{q})\mathrm{Id}-R_{\ell}\right)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{(\partial_{t}+\widehat{u}_{q}\cdot\nabla)w_{q+1}+w_{q+1}\cdot\nabla\widehat{u}_{q}}_{=:\,\mathrm{div}S_{TN}}+\underbrace{\mathrm{div}\left(w_{q+1,h}\otimes w_{q+1,h}\right)}_{=:\mathrm{div}S_{O,h}}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\mathrm{div}\left(w^{(p)}_{q+1,R}\otimes w^{(p)}_{q+1,R}-P_{q+1}\mathrm{Id}+R_{\ell}\right)}_{=:\,\mathrm{div}S_{O,R}}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\mathrm{div}\left(w^{(p)}_{q+1,R}\otimes_{s}w^{(c)}_{q+1,R}+w^{(c)}_{q+1,R}\otimes w^{(c)}_{q+1,R}\right)}_{=:\,\mathrm{div}S_{C}}+\underbrace{\mathrm{div}\left(R_{q}^{q}-\frac{1}{3}(\mathrm{tr\,}R_{q}^{q})\mathrm{Id}-R_{\ell}\right)}_{=:\mathrm{div}S_{M1}}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{(\partial_{t}+\widehat{u}_{q}\cdot\nabla)(\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}-w_{q+1})+\mathrm{div}((\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}-w_{q+1})\otimes\widehat{u}_{q}+\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}\otimes\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}-w_{q+1}\otimes w_{q+1})}_{=:\mathrm{div}S_{M2}}\,.

(7.1)

Here
P_{q+1}
will be specified later in section
7.2
. Note that we used (
5.40
) to replace
u_{q}
with
\widehat{u}_{q}
in the second equality. Also, we have that
\partial_{t}w_{q+1}+(\widehat{u}_{q}\cdot\nabla)w_{q+1}+w_{q+1}\cdot\nabla\widehat{u}_{q}
has zero mean, and so it can be written in divergence form
\mathrm{div}S_{TN}
. The same statement holds for
\mathrm{div}S_{M2}
. We set

R_{q+1}=R_{q}-R_{q}^{q}+S_{q+1}\,,\quad p_{q+1}=p_{q}-\frac{1}{3}(\mathrm{tr\,}R_{q}^{q})+P_{q+1}\,.

(7.2)

We then have that
(u_{q+1},p_{q+1},R_{q+1})
solves the Euler-Reynolds system

\displaystyle\partial_{t}u_{q+1}+\mathrm{div}\left(u_{q+1}\otimes u_{q+1}\right)+\nabla p_{q+1}=\mathrm{div}(R_{q+1})\,,\qquad\mathrm{div}\,u_{q+1}=0\,.

(7.3)

We will prove that the new stress error
S_{q+1}
can be decomposed into components
{S}_{q+1}^{k}
as
S_{q+1}=\sum_{k=q+1}^{q+{\bar{n}}}{S}_{q+1}^{k}
, each of which satisfies estimates consistent with the inductive assumptions.

7.2 Oscillation error

Lemma 7.1

(
Oscillation error from
w_{q+1,h}
)
.

The symmetric stress
S_{O,h}:=w_{q+1,h}\otimes w_{q+1,h}
satisfies the following estimates for
N,M\leq\frac{\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}{10}
:

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{i,q}D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}S_{O,h}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{q+2{\bar{n}}}\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\mu_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+13},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{8}\right)\,,

(7.4a)

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{i,q}D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}S_{O,h}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\mu_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+13},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{8}\right)\,.

(7.4b)

Proof.

We appeal to Corollary
5.14
; for the
L^{1}
estimate, we use (
5.44c
) and (
5.45c
) with
r=2
, the former of which gives the weakest estimate. Fixing values of
i,k,\vec{l},I
and recalling the notation from (
5.31
), we have that for
N,M\leq\frac{\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}{10}
,

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\left(w_{{(\xi)}}^{(h),I}\otimes w_{{(\xi)}}^{(h),I}\right)\right\|_{1}\lesssim\left|\mathrm{supp\,}\left(\eta_{i,k,\xi,\vec{l}}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I}\right)\right|\frac{\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{2}}{\lambda_{q*}^{3}}\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\mu_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+13},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{8}\right)\,.

(7.5)

Upon applying the usual aggregation lemmas and parameter inequality (
8.5f
), we obtain (
7.4a
). In order to obtain (
7.4b
), we apply (
5.44d
), which essentially asserts that the
L^{\infty}
norm of
w_{(\xi)}^{(h),I}\otimes w_{(\xi)}^{(h),I}
is
\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{2}\lambda_{q*}^{-3}r_{q}^{-2}
. This quantity is much less than
1
since there is an extra
\lambda_{q*}
in the denominator, and the remainder
\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{2}\lambda_{q*}^{-2}r_{q}^{-2}
is of order
b-1
. Derivative estimates follow from Corollary
5.14
, and we omit further details. ∎

We now consider the oscillation error
S_{O,R}
from
w_{q+1,R}
.
In order to define it, we first consider

\displaystyle\mathrm{div}\left(w^{(p)}_{q+1,R}\otimes w^{(p)}_{q+1,R}\right)^{\bullet}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l}}\partial_{\alpha}\left(a_{(\xi)}(\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1})_{\theta}^{\alpha}\mathbb{B}_{(\xi)}^{\theta}(\Phi_{(i,k)})\,a_{(\xi)}(\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1})_{\gamma}^{\bullet}\mathbb{B}_{(\xi)}^{\gamma}(\Phi_{(i,k)})\right)\,,

(7.6)

where
\bullet
denotes the unspecified components of a vector field and we have used (
5.41
) from Lemma
5.11
to eliminate all cross terms. Recalling from (
5.21
) that

\mathbb{B}_{(\xi)}=\boldsymbol{\rho}_{(\xi)}\sum_{I}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I}\mathbb{W}_{(\xi)}^{I}

and using (
3.7
),
item (
3
) of Proposition
4.9, []
, and
item (
4
) of Proposition
4.5, []
, we decompose

\displaystyle(\mathbb{B}\otimes\mathbb{B})_{(\xi)}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\rho}_{(\xi)}^{2}\sum_{I}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I})^{2}\mathbb{P}_{\neq 0}(\mathbb{W}_{(\xi)}^{I}\otimes\mathbb{W}_{(\xi)}^{I})+\mathbb{P}_{\neq 0}\boldsymbol{\rho}_{(\xi)}^{2}\xi\otimes\xi+\xi\otimes\xi.

(7.7)

Since the vector field used to define the simple symmetric tensors in (
7.7
) does not vary in the
\xi
-direction, the symmetric tensor satisfies
\xi\cdot\nabla(\mathbb{B}\otimes\mathbb{B})_{{(\xi)}}=0
. Then using the identity
\partial_{\alpha}((\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1})_{\theta}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{B}\otimes\mathbb{B})_{{(\xi)}}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\xi^{\theta})=\xi^{\theta}(\partial_{\theta}(\mathbb{B}\otimes\mathbb{B})_{{(\xi)}})\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}=0
, (
7.6
) can be expanded as

\displaystyle\mathrm{div}\left(w^{(p)}_{q+1,R}\otimes w^{(p)}_{q+1,R}\right)^{\bullet}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l}}\partial_{\alpha}\left(a_{(\xi)}^{2}(\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1})_{\theta}^{\alpha}(\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1})_{\gamma}^{\bullet}(\xi^{\theta}\xi^{\gamma})\right)

(7.8a)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l}}B_{(\xi)}^{\bullet}\left(\mathbb{P}_{\neq 0}\boldsymbol{\rho}_{(\xi)}^{2}\right)\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}

(7.8b)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l}}B_{(\xi)}^{\bullet}\left(\boldsymbol{\rho}_{(\xi)}^{2}\sum_{I}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I})^{2}\mathbb{P}_{\neq 0}(\varrho_{(\xi),R}^{I})^{2}\right)\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}

(7.8c)

where

B_{(\xi),\diamond}^{\bullet}:=\xi^{\theta}\xi^{\gamma}\partial_{\alpha}\left(a_{(\xi)}^{2}(\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1})_{\theta}^{\alpha}(\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1})_{\gamma}^{\bullet}\right)\,,\qquad\varrho_{(\xi),R}^{I}=\xi\cdot\mathbb{W}_{(\xi)}^{I}\,.

(7.9)

The first term (
7.8a
) cancels out
\mathrm{div}R_{\ell}
from (
7.1
) up to the addition of a potential
P_{q+1}
:

\displaystyle\sum_{\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l}}a_{(\xi)}^{2}\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1}\left(\xi\otimes\xi\right)\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-\top}\underset{\eqref{eq:a:xi:def}}{=}\sum_{\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l}}e_{q+1}\Gamma_{q}^{2j}\eta_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l}}^{2}\gamma_{\xi}^{2}\left(\frac{R_{q,i,j,k}}{e_{q+1}\Gamma_{q}^{2j}}\right)\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1}\left(\xi\otimes\xi\right)\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-\top}

\displaystyle\quad\underset{\eqref{def:cumulative:current},\eqref{eq:rqnpj},\eqref{eq:summy:summ:1}}{=}\sum_{i,j,k}e_{q+1}\psi_{i,q}^{2}\omega_{j,q}^{2}\chi_{i,k,q}^{2}\Gamma_{q}^{2j}\mathrm{Id}-R_{\ell}=:P_{q+1}\mathrm{Id}-R_{\ell}\,.

(7.10)

The inverse divergence of the remaining terms (
7.8b
)-(
7.8c
) will therefore form the oscillation errors.

Lemma 7.2

(
Oscillation error from
w_{q+1,R}
)
.

There exist symmetric stresses
S_{O,R}^{m}
for
m=1,\dots,q+{\bar{n}}
such that the following hold.

(i)

For the potential
P_{q+1}
defined as in (
7.10
), we have
\mathrm{div}\left(w_{q+1}^{(p)}\otimes w_{q+1}^{(p)}-P_{q+1}\mathrm{Id}+R_{\ell}\right)=\sum_{m=q+1}^{q+{\bar{n}}}\mathrm{div}S_{O,R}^{m}
.

(ii)

For
m=q+1,\dots,q+{\bar{n}}
and
N+M\leq 2\mathsf{N}_{\rm ind}
, the errors
{S}^{m}_{O,R}
satisfy

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{i,m-1}D^{N}D_{t,m-1}^{M}{S}^{m}_{O,R}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{m}^{-1}\delta_{m+{\bar{n}}}\Lambda_{m}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\mu_{m-1}^{-1}\Gamma_{m-1}^{i+20},\mathrm{T}_{m-1}^{-1}\Gamma_{m-1}^{10}\right)

(7.11a)

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{i,m-1}D^{N}D_{t,m-1}^{M}{S}^{m}_{O,R}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{m}^{\mathsf{C_{\infty}}-1}\Lambda_{m}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\mu_{m-1}^{-1}\Gamma_{m-1}^{i+20},\mathrm{T}_{m-1}^{-1}\Gamma_{m-1}^{10}\right)\,.

(7.11b)

Proof.

The proof essentially follows from that of
[
29
, Lemma 8.1]
.
Applying
[
29
, Proposition A.13]
, the inverse divergence of (
7.8b
) will define
S_{O,R}^{q+1}
. Also, we decompose (
7.8c
) using the synthetic Littlewood-Paley decomposition (see
[
29
, Section 4.3]
), then the inverse divergence of each piece will generate
S_{O,R}^{m}
for
m=q+\nicefrac{{{\bar{n}}}}{{2}}+1,\cdots,q+{\bar{n}}
. The undefined
S_{O,R}^{m}
are set to be zero. By (
7.8
) and (
7.10
), item (
i
) holds.

To estimate
S^{m}_{q+1}
, we apply
[
29
, Proposition A.13]
and Corollary
5.9
, and use Lemma
5.13
. In our applications of these estimates, the parameter choices are essentially the same as in
[
29
, Lemma 8.1]
except that here we estimate in
L^{1}
instead of
L^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}
. With suitable modifications, we obtain (
7.11
) for
m=q+1
:

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{i,q}D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}{S}^{q+1}_{O,R}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda_{q}\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}}\Gamma_{q}^{56}\lambda_{q+1}^{-1+\alpha+N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\mu_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+14},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{9}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q+1}^{-9}\delta_{q+1+{\bar{n}}}\lambda_{q+1}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\mu_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+14},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{9}\right)

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{i,q}D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}{S}^{q+1}_{O,R}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q+1}^{\mathsf{C_{\infty}}-9}\lambda_{q+1}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\mu_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+14},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{9}\right)

for
N,M\leq 2\mathsf{N}_{\rm ind}
,
where
\alpha
is a constant arbitrarily close to
0
(see the choice of
\alpha
in item
viii
).

For
m=q+\nicefrac{{{\bar{n}}}}{{2}}+1,\cdots,q+{\bar{n}}
,
S_{O,R}^{m}
have a decomposition
S_{O,R}^{m}=S_{O,R}^{m,l}+S_{O,R}^{m,*}
such that the following holds. In the case of
m=q+\nicefrac{{{\bar{n}}}}{{2}}+1
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{i,q}D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}{S}^{q+\nicefrac{{{\bar{n}}}}{{2}}+1,l}_{O,R}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda_{q}\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}}\Gamma_{q}^{55}\lambda_{q+\nicefrac{{{\bar{n}}}}{{2}}+1}^{-1+N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\mu_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+14},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{9}\right)

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{i,q}D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}{S}^{q+\nicefrac{{{\bar{n}}}}{{2}}+1,l}_{O,R}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q+\nicefrac{{{\bar{n}}}}{{2}}+1}^{\mathsf{C_{\infty}}-9}\lambda_{q+\nicefrac{{{\bar{n}}}}{{2}}+1}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\mu_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+14},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{9}\right)\,,

and otherwise

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{i,q}D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}{S}^{m,l}_{O,R}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda_{q}\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}}\Gamma_{q}^{56}\lambda_{m-1}^{-2}\lambda_{m}^{N+1}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\mu_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+14},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{9}\right)

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{i,q}D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}{S}^{m,l}_{O,R}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{m}^{\mathsf{C_{\infty}}-9}\lambda_{m}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\mu_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+14},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{9}\right)\,

for
N,M\leq 2\mathsf{N}_{\rm ind}
. Here the
L^{\infty}
-norm estimates follows from (
8.6
). Furthermore,
S_{O,R}^{m,l}
and
S_{O,R}^{m,*}
satisfy that for
m=q+\nicefrac{{{\bar{n}}}}{{2}}+1,\cdots,q+{\bar{n}}
,
q+1\leq q^{\prime}\leq m-1
, and
N,M\leq 2\mathsf{N}_{\rm ind}
,

\displaystyle B(\mathrm{supp\,}\widehat{w}_{q^{\prime}},\lambda_{q^{\prime}}^{-1}\Gamma_{q^{\prime}+1})\cap\mathrm{supp\,}S_{O}^{m,l}=\emptyset\,,\qquad\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}{S}^{m,*}_{O,R}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\delta_{q+3{\bar{n}}}\mathrm{T}_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{4\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}}}\lambda_{m}^{N}\mu_{q}^{-M}\,,

where the latter inequality follows from item
xv
.
Combining these outcomes with Lemma
9.1
and using (
8.5b
) and (
8.5c
), we obtain the desired estimates (
7.11
) for the remaining cases of
m
.
∎

7.3 Transport and Nash errors

Lemma 7.3

(
Transport/Nash error).

There exist symmetric stresses
S_{TN}
such that

(i)

\mathrm{div}S_{TN}=(\partial_{t}+\widehat{u}_{q}\cdot\nabla)w_{q+1}+w_{q+1}\cdot\nabla\widehat{u}_{q}
,

(ii)

and for all
N+M\leq 2\mathsf{N}_{\rm ind}
, the error
S_{TN}
satisfies

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{i,q}D^{N}D_{t,q+{\bar{n}}-1}^{M}S_{TN}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{-1}\delta_{q+2{\bar{n}}}\Lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\mu_{q+{\bar{n}}-1}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}-1}^{i+20},\mathrm{T}_{q+{\bar{n}}-1}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}-1}^{10}\right)

(7.12)

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{i,q}D^{N}D_{t,q+{\bar{n}}-1}^{M}S_{TN}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{\mathsf{C_{\infty}}-1}\Lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\mu_{q+{\bar{n}}-1}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}-1}^{i+20},\mathrm{T}_{q+{\bar{n}}-1}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}-1}^{10}\right)\,.

Proof.

The proof follows from the same argument as in
[
29
, Lemma 8.6]
, but incorporates the helicity corrector instead of the current corrector. Since
D_{t,q}w_{q+1}
and
w_{q+1}\cdot\nabla\widehat{u}_{q}
are mean-zero, we apply
[
29
, Proposition A.13]
to define
S_{TN}
, verifying (
i
). Furthermore, by
[
29
, Proposition A.13]
, Corollary
5.9
, Lemma
5.13
, (
8.5a
), (
8.6
) and
item
xv
,
S_{TN}=S_{TN}^{l}+S_{TN}^{*}
, where
S_{TN}^{l}
and
S_{TN}^{*}
satisfy that for any
N,M\leq 2\mathsf{N}_{\rm ind}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{i,q}D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}S_{TN}^{l}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q}^{{\mathsf{C}_{b}}+53}\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{\frac{1}{2}}r_{q}\mu_{q}^{-1}\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{-1+N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\mu_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+15},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{9}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{-100}\delta_{q+2{\bar{n}}}\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\mu_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+15},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{9}\right)

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{i,q}D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}S_{TN}^{l}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{\mathsf{C_{\infty}}-200}\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\mu_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+15},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{9}\right)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}S_{TN}^{*}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{q+3{\bar{n}}}^{2}\mathrm{T}_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{4\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}}}\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{N}\mu_{q}^{-M}\,,

and for any
q+1\leq q^{\prime}\leq q+{\bar{n}}-1
,
B(\mathrm{supp\,}\widehat{w}_{q^{\prime}},\lambda_{q^{\prime}}^{-1}\Gamma_{q^{\prime}+1})\cap\mathrm{supp\,}S_{TN}^{l}=\emptyset
. Here, the error from helicity corrector does not change the upper bound, since
\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}\lambda_{q*}^{-3/2}\leq\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{1/2}
(see (
8.5f
)). The estimates in (
ii
) then follow from Lemma
9.1
.
∎

7.4 Divergence corrector error

Recalling (
7.1
), we write
S_{C}=S_{C1}+S_{C2}
, where

\mathrm{div}S_{C1}=\mathrm{div}\left(w_{q+1,R}^{(p)}\otimes_{s}w_{q+1,R}^{(c)}\right)\,,\qquad S_{C2}=w_{q+1,R}^{(c)}\otimes w_{q+1,R}^{(c)}\,.

(7.13)

Lemma 7.4

(
Divergence corrector error from
w_{q+1,R}
).

There exist symmetric stresses
S_{C}^{m}
for
m=q+\nicefrac{{{\bar{n}}}}{{2}}+1,\cdots,q+{\bar{n}}
such that the following hold.

(i)

\mathrm{div}\left(w_{q+1,R}^{(p)}\otimes_{s}w_{q+1,R}^{(c)}+w_{q+1}^{(c)}\otimes w_{q+1}^{(c)}\right)=\sum_{m=q+\lfloor\nicefrac{{{\bar{n}}}}{{2}}\rfloor+1}^{q+{\bar{n}}}\mathrm{div}S_{C}^{m}
.

(ii)

For the same range of
m
and for all
N+M\leq 2\mathsf{N}_{\rm ind}
, the errors satisfy

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{i,m-1}D^{N}D_{t,m-1}^{M}S_{C}^{m}\right\|_{1}\lesssim\Gamma_{m}^{-1}\delta_{m+{\bar{n}}}\Lambda_{m}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\mu_{m-1}^{-1}\Gamma_{m-1}^{i+20},\mathrm{T}_{m-1}^{-1}\Gamma_{m-1}^{10}\right)

(7.14a)

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{i,m-1}D^{N}D_{t,m-1}^{M}S_{C}^{m}\right\|_{\infty}\lesssim\Gamma_{m}^{-1}\Lambda_{m}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\mu_{m-1}^{-1}\Gamma_{m-1}^{i+20},\mathrm{T}_{m-1}^{-1}\Gamma_{m-1}^{10}\right)\,.

(7.14b)

Proof.

We first define
S_{C}^{m}
as in
Step 2
of the proof of
[
29
, Lemma 8.10]
; For
S_{C1}
, we expand
\mathrm{div}\left(w_{q+1,R}^{(p)}\otimes_{s}w_{q+1,R}^{(c)}\right)
,
decompose them using the synthetic Littlewood-Paley decomposition, and take the inverse divergence applying
[
29
, Proposition A.13]
to define
S_{C1}^{m}
. For
S_{C2}
, on the other hand, the term
w_{q+1,R}^{(c)}\otimes w_{q+1,R}^{(c)}
directly goes into
S_{C2}^{q+{\bar{n}}}
. Then, we set
S_{C}^{m}:=S_{C1}^{m}+S_{C2}^{m}
, where all undefined
S_{C1}^{m}
and
S_{C2}^{m}
are identically zero. Then, the estimates for
S_{C}^{m}
are obtained essentially by the same argument as in the proof of
[
29
, Lemma 8.10]
and Lemma
9.1
with suitable modifications to replace
L^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}
-norm estimate by
L^{1}
-norm estimate.
∎

7.5 Mollification errors

Recalling from subsection
7.1
that
\mathrm{div}S_{M2}
has mean-zero, we use
[
29
, Proposition A.13]
,
[
29
, Remank A.15]
to first define the mollification error
S_{M}=S_{M1}+S_{M2}
by

\displaystyle S_{M1}

\displaystyle:=R_{q}^{q}-\nicefrac{{1}}{{3}}(\mathrm{tr\,}R_{q}^{q})\mathrm{Id}-R_{\ell}

(7.15)

\displaystyle S_{M2}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{R}^{*}\left[(\partial_{t}+\widehat{u}_{q}\cdot\nabla)(\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}-w_{q+1})\right]+(\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}-w_{q+1})\otimes\widehat{u}_{q}+\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}\otimes\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}-w_{q+1}\otimes w_{q+1}\,.

Lemma 7.5

(
Mollification error
S_{M}
).

For all
N+M\leq 2\mathsf{N}_{\rm ind}
,

S_{M}
satisfies

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}S_{M1}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\Gamma_{q+1}^{9}\delta_{q+3{\bar{n}}}\mathrm{T}_{q+1}^{2\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}}}\left(\lambda_{q+1}\Gamma_{q+1}\right)^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\mu_{q}^{-1},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\right)\,,

(7.16a)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q+{\bar{n}}-1}^{M}S_{M2}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{9}\delta_{q+3{\bar{n}}}\mathrm{T}_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{2\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}}}\left(\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}}\right)^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q+{\bar{n}}-1}^{-1},\mathrm{T}_{q+{\bar{n}}-1}^{-1}\right)\,.

(7.16b)

Proof of Lemma
7.5
.

From item
iv
from Lemma
4.1
, we have that for all
N+M\leq 2\mathsf{N}_{\rm ind}
,

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}S_{M}\right\|_{\infty}\lesssim\Gamma_{q+1}\mathrm{T}_{q+1}^{2\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}}}\delta_{q+3{\bar{n}}}^{2}\lambda_{q+1}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\mu_{q}^{-1},\Gamma_{q}^{-1}\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\right)\,,

which immediately leads to (
7.16a
). To deal with
S_{M2}
, we recall from (
5.47
) that

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q+{\bar{n}}-1}^{M}\left(w_{q+1}-\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}\right)\right\|_{\infty}\lesssim\delta_{q+3{\bar{n}}}^{3}\mathrm{T}_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{25\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}}}\left(\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}-1}\right)^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q+{\bar{n}}-1}^{-1},\mathrm{T}_{q+{\bar{n}}-1}^{-1}\right)\,.

for all
N+M\leq\nicefrac{{\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{4}}
. Using Lemma
5.11
, we note that
D_{t,q-{\bar{n}}-1}w_{q+1}=D_{t,q}w_{q+1}
and
D_{t,q-{\bar{n}}-1}\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}=D_{t,q}\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}
. Then, writing
\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}\otimes\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}-w_{q+1}\otimes w_{q+1}=(\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}-w_{q+1})\otimes\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}+w_{q+1}\otimes(\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}-w_{q+1})
and using (
5.46
) and (
5.47
),
we have that for all
N+M\leq 2\mathsf{N}_{\rm ind}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{i,q+{\bar{n}}-1}D^{N}D_{t,q+{\bar{n}}-1}^{M}[\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}\otimes\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}-w_{q+1}\otimes w_{q+1}]\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\delta_{q+3{\bar{n}}}\mathrm{T}_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{2\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}}}\left(\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}}\right)^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q+{\bar{n}}-1}^{-1},\mathrm{T}_{q+{\bar{n}}-1}^{-1}\right)\,.

(7.17)

As for the remaining term, we first upgrade the material derivative in the estimate for
\widehat{u}_{q}
.
Applying Lemma
9.1
to
F^{l}=0
,
F^{*}=\widehat{u}_{q}
,
k=q+{\bar{n}}
,
N_{\star}=\nicefrac{{3\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{4}}
with (
2.25a
), we get

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q+{\bar{n}}-1}^{M}\widehat{u}_{q}\right\|_{\infty}\lesssim\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{N}\mathrm{T}_{q+{\bar{n}}-1}^{-M}

Here, we used (
8.7
). Then, we use
[
29
, Remank A.15]
with (
8.7
), setting

\displaystyle G

\displaystyle=D_{t,q+{\bar{n}}-1}(\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}-w_{q+1})\quad(\text{or }G=(\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}-w_{q+1})\otimes\widehat{u}_{q}),\quad v=\widehat{u}_{q+{\bar{n}}-1}

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{G,\infty}

\displaystyle=\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{3}\mathrm{T}_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{20\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}}},\quad\lambda=\lambda^{\prime}=\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}-1},\quad M_{t}=\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\quad\nu=\nu^{\prime}=\mathrm{T}_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{-1},\quad\mathcal{C}_{v}=\Lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}-1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055ptN_{*}=\nicefrac{{\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{9}},\quad M_{*}=\nicefrac{{\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{10}},\quad N_{\circ}=M_{\circ}=2\mathsf{N}_{\rm ind}\,.

As a result, with a suitable choice of positive integer
K_{\circ}
so that
\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{3}\mathrm{T}_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{20\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}}}\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{5}2^{2\mathsf{N}_{\rm ind}}\leq\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{-K_{\circ}}\leq\delta_{q+3{\bar{n}}}\mathrm{T}_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{10\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}}}
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q+{\bar{n}}-1}^{M}\mathcal{R}^{*}(D_{t,q}(\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}-w_{q+1}))\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q+{\bar{n}}-1}^{M}\mathcal{R}^{*}(D_{t,q+{\bar{n}}-1}(\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}-w_{q+1}))\right\|_{\infty}

(7.18)

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{q+3{\bar{n}}}\mathrm{T}_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{10\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}}}(\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}})^{N}\mathrm{T}_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{-M}

\displaystyle\leq\delta_{q+3{\bar{n}}}\mathrm{T}_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{2\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}}}(\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}})^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{-1},\mathrm{T}_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{-1}\right)\,,

for all
N+M\leq 2\mathsf{N}_{\rm ind}
. This completes the proof of (
7.16b
).
∎

8
Parameters

In this section, we specify parameters and list some resulting inequalities, none of which depend on
q
. We note that we choose
a
last, for which
\lambda_{q}
and
\delta_{q}
are defined as in (
2.5a
); as a consequence,
a^{(b^{q})}\leq\lambda_{q}\leq 2a^{(b^{q})}
and
\frac{1}{3}\lambda_{q}^{b}\leq\lambda_{q+1}\leq 2\lambda_{q}^{b}
.

(i)

Choose
\overline{b}
as in item (
i
).

(ii)

Choose
\beta
as in item (
ii
). The upper bound
\beta<\nicefrac{{1}}{{2\overline{b}}}
is needed in the oscillation error estimates and guarantees

\displaystyle\lambda_{q}\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}}(\lambda_{m}\delta_{m+{\bar{n}}})^{-1}\ll 1

(8.1)

for any integer
m>q
. The lower bound
\beta>\frac{2+\overline{b}-2\overline{b}^{2}}{1+3\overline{b}^{2}-2\overline{b}^{4}}
comes from the transport error estimate, where we need

\displaystyle\lambda_{q}^{2}\delta_{q}^{\frac{1}{2}}\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{\frac{3}{2}}{\lambda_{q+\frac{{\bar{n}}}{2}}}{\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{-2}}\ll\delta_{q+2{\bar{n}}}\,.

(8.2)

Lastly, we imposed the other lower bound
\beta>\frac{2-\overline{b}}{2\overline{b}}
to guarantee

\displaystyle\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}}\lambda_{q+\nicefrac{{{\bar{n}}}}{{2}}}^{2}\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{-1}\ll 1\,.

(8.3)

This is required for the estimate of the helicity generated by Euler-Reynolds principal-corrector.

(iii)

Choose an even integer
{\bar{n}}\gg 1
as in (
iii
), so that
\overline{b}^{2}<2
and (
2.3
) implies that

\displaystyle\lambda_{q}\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}}\lambda_{m-1}^{-2}\lambda_{m}\ll\delta_{m+{\bar{n}}}

(8.4)

for
m=q+\nicefrac{{{\bar{n}}}}{{2}}+1,\cdots,q+{\bar{n}}
, which is required for the oscillation error estimates.

(iv)

Consequently, for
q^{\prime}\geq q-\nicefrac{{{\bar{n}}}}{{2}}+1
,
\lambda_{q^{\prime}+\nicefrac{{{\bar{n}}}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+\nicefrac{{{\bar{n}}}}{{2}}}\left(\lambda_{q}\lambda_{q^{\prime}+{\bar{n}}}\right)^{-1}<1
.

(v)

Choose

.

(vi)

)-(

\displaystyle\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}r_{q}\mu_{q}^{-1}\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{1000}

\displaystyle\underset{\eqref{par:trans:pre}}{<}\delta_{q+2{\bar{n}}}

(8.5a)

\displaystyle\lambda_{q}\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}}(\lambda_{m}\delta_{m+{\bar{n}}})^{-1}\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{1000}

\displaystyle\underset{\eqref{par:osc:pre3}}{<}1\quad\text{for }m>q

\displaystyle<1

(8.11a)
