9
Appendix

In this section, we collect a number of useful tools from
[
6
]
and
[
29
]
.

9.1 Upgrading material derivatives

We now recall a version of
[
29
, Lemma A.23]
, which upgrades material derivatives for functions decomposed into a “localized” piece with support assumptions and a “nonlocal” remainder. The only difference is the substitution of
\tau
’a for
\mu
’s.

Lemma 9.1

(
Upgrading material derivatives).

Fix
p\in[1,\infty]
and a positive integer
N_{\star}\leq\nicefrac{{3\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{4}}
. Assume that a tensor
F
is given with a decomposition
F=F^{l}+F^{*}
such that

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{i,q^{\prime}}D^{N}D_{t,q^{\prime}}^{M}F^{l}\right\|_{p}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{C}_{p,F}\lambda_{F}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q^{\prime}}^{i+c}{\mu_{q^{\prime}}^{-1}},\Gamma_{q^{\prime}}^{-1}\mathrm{T}_{q^{\prime}}^{-1}\right)

(9.1a)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q^{\prime}}^{M}F^{*}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{C}_{*,F}\mathrm{T}_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}}}\lambda_{F}^{N}{\mu_{q^{\prime}}}^{-M}

(9.1b)

for all
M+N\leq N_{\star}
, an absolute constant
c\leq 20
, and constants
\mathcal{C}_{p,F}
and
\mathcal{C}_{*,F}
. Assume furthermore that there exists
k
such that
q^{\prime}+1<k\leq q^{\prime}+{\bar{n}}
and

\displaystyle\mathrm{supp\,}(\widehat{w}_{q^{\prime\prime}},\lambda_{q^{\prime\prime}}^{-1}{\Gamma_{q^{\prime\prime}}})\cap\mathrm{supp\,}(F^{l})=\emptyset\quad\forall q^{\prime}+1\leq q^{\prime\prime}<k\,.

(9.2)

Finally, assume that
\lambda_{F}\Gamma_{q^{\prime}+{\bar{n}}}^{{i_{\rm max}}+2}\delta_{q^{\prime}+{\bar{n}}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\leq\mathrm{T}_{q^{\prime}+{\bar{n}}}^{-1}
. Then
F
obeys the following estimate with an upgraded material derivative for all
M+N\leq N_{\star}
;

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{i,k-1}D^{N}D_{t,k-1}^{M}F\right\|_{p}\lesssim\ (\mathcal{C}_{p,F}+\mathcal{C}_{*,F})\max(\lambda_{F},\Lambda_{k-1})^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{k-1}^{i}{\mu}_{k-1}^{-1},\Gamma_{k-1}^{-1}\mathrm{T}_{k-1}^{-1}\right)\,.

In particular, the nonlocal part
F^{*}
obeys for
N+M\leq N_{\star}
the better estimate

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,k-1}^{M}F^{*}\right\|_{\infty}\lesssim\mathcal{C}_{*,F}\max(\lambda_{F},\lambda_{k-1}\Gamma_{k-1})^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},{\mu}_{k-1}^{-1},\mathrm{T}_{k-1}^{-1}\Gamma_{k-1}^{-1}\right)\,.

(9.3)

9.2 Velocity cutoffs

We must define the velocity cutoffs in order to verify the assumption in subsection
2.3
and
2.6
. The proofs are quite similar to those in
[
29
,
6
,
45
]
, and so many details are omitted. The main differences are cosmetic; most of the
\tau
’s have been replaced with
\mu
’s.

9.2.1 Mollified velocity increment and velocity cutoff functions

We recall the definition of
\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}
from (
5.33
);
\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}=\mathcal{\widetilde{P}}_{q+{\bar{n}},x,t}w_{q+1}
. Next, we shall need the following.

Definition 9.2

(
Translating
\Gamma
’s between
q^{\prime}-1
and
q^{\prime}
).

Given
i,j,q^{\prime}\geq 0
, we define
i_{*}=i_{*}(j,q^{\prime})=i_{*}(j)=\min\{i\geq 0\colon\Gamma_{q^{\prime}}^{i}\geq\Gamma_{q^{\prime}-1}^{j}\}
and
j_{*}(i,q^{\prime})=\max\{j:i_{*}(j)\leq i\}
. A consequence of these definition is the inequality
\Gamma_{q^{\prime}}^{i-1}<\Gamma_{q^{\prime}-1}^{j_{*}(i,q)}\leq\Gamma_{q^{\prime}}^{i}
. We also note that for
j=0
,
i_{*}(j)=0
. Finally, the upper bound for
i_{*}(j)
depends on
j
but not
q
.

We now define the velocity cutoff functions using the cutoff functions from
[
29
, Lemma 5.5]
and
[
6
, Lemma 6.2]
, analogously to
[
29
, Definition 9.2]
.

Definition 9.3

(
Intermediate cutoffs).

For
{q+{\bar{n}}}\geq 1
,
m\leq\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}
, and
j_{m}\geq 0
, we define

\displaystyle h_{m,j_{m},q+{\bar{n}}}^{2}(x,t)

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{-2i_{*}(j_{m})}\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{-1}\left({\mu_{q+{\bar{n}}-1}^{-1}}\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{i_{*}(j_{m})+2}\right)^{-2m}\sum_{N=0}^{\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}}\left(\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}}\right)^{-2N}\left|D^{N}D_{t,q+{\bar{n}}-1}^{m}\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}\right|^{2}\,.

(9.4)

We then define
\psi_{m,i_{m},j_{m},q+{\bar{n}}}
by

\displaystyle\psi_{m,i_{m},j_{m},q+{\bar{n}}}(x,t)

\displaystyle=\gamma_{m,q+{\bar{n}}}^{3}\left(\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{-2(i_{m}-i_{*}(j_{m}))(m+1)}h_{m,j_{m},q+{\bar{n}}}^{2}(x,t)\right)\,\qquad i_{m}>i_{*}(j_{m})\,,

(9.5a)

\displaystyle\psi_{m,i_{*}(j_{m}),j_{m},q+{\bar{n}}}(x,t)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\gamma}_{m,q+{\bar{n}}}^{3}\left(h_{m,j_{m},q+{\bar{n}}}^{2}(x,t)\right)\qquad i_{m}=i_{*}(j_{m})\,.

(9.5b)

The intermediate cutoff functions
\psi_{m,i_{m},j_{m},q+{\bar{n}}}
are equal to zero for
i_{m}<i_{*}(j_{m})
.

Similar to
[
29
, equations (9.7), (9.8)]
, it follows that
\sum_{i_{m}\geq 0}\psi_{m,i_{m},j_{m},q+{\bar{n}}}^{2}=\sum_{i_{m}\geq i_{*}(j_{m})}\psi_{m,i_{m},j_{m},q+{\bar{n}}}^{2}=\sum_{\{i_{m}\colon\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{i_{m}}\geq\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}-1}^{j_{m}}\}}\psi_{m,i_{m},j_{m},q+{\bar{n}}}^{2}\equiv 1

for any
m
, and for
|i_{m}-i^{\prime}_{m}|\geq 2
,
\psi_{m,i_{m},j_{m},q+{\bar{n}}}\psi_{m,i_{m}^{\prime},j_{m},q+{\bar{n}}}=0
.

Definition 9.4

(
m^{\textnormal{th}}
Velocity Cutoff Function).

At stage
q+1
and for
i_{m}\geq 0
, we define

\psi_{m,i_{m},q+{\bar{n}}}^{2}=\sum\limits_{\{j_{m}\colon i_{m}\geq i_{*}(j_{m})\}}\psi_{j_{m},q+{\bar{n}}-1}^{2}\psi_{m,i_{m},j_{m},q+{\bar{n}}}^{2}\,.

(9.6)

We shall employ the notation
\vec{i}=\{i_{m}\}_{m=0}^{\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}}=\left(i_{0},...,i_{\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}}\right)\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}+1}
to signify a tuple of non-negative integers of length
\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}+1
.

Definition 9.5

(
Velocity cutoff function).

At stage
q+1
and for
0\leq i\leq i_{\rm max}
, we define

\displaystyle\psi_{i,q+{\bar{n}}}^{2}=\sum_{\mathcal{I}}\prod_{m=0}^{\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}}\psi_{m,i_{m},q+{\bar{n}}}^{2}\,,\qquad\mathcal{I}=\left\{\vec{i}\colon\max\limits_{0\leq m\leq\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}}i_{m}=i\right\}\,.

(9.7)

For
\vec{i}
as in the sum of (
9.7
), we shall denote

\displaystyle\mathrm{supp\,}\left(\prod\limits_{m=0}^{\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}}\psi_{m,i_{m},q+{\bar{n}}}\right)=\bigcap_{m=0}^{\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}}\mathrm{supp\,}(\psi_{m,i_{m},q+{\bar{n}}})=:\mathrm{supp\,}(\psi_{\vec{i},q+{\bar{n}}})\,,

(9.8)

so that
(x,t)\in\mathrm{supp\,}(\psi_{i,q+{\bar{n}}})
if and only if there exists
\vec{i}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}+1}
such that
\max_{0\leq m\leq\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}}i_{m}=i
, and
(x,t)\in\mathrm{supp\,}(\psi_{\vec{i},q+{\bar{n}}})
.

9.2.2 Partition of unity, dodging, simple bounds on increments, and spatial derivatives

Lemma 9.6

(
Partition of unity).

For all
m
,
\sum_{i_{m}\geq 0}\psi_{m,i_{m},q+{\bar{n}}}^{2}\equiv 1
and
\psi_{m,i_{m},q+{\bar{n}}}\psi_{m,i^{\prime}_{m},q+{\bar{n}}}=0
for
|i_{m}-i^{\prime}_{m}|\geq 2
. In addition, we have that
\sum_{i\geq 0}\psi_{i,q+{\bar{n}}}^{2}\equiv 1
and
\psi_{i,q+{\bar{n}}}\psi_{i^{\prime},q+{\bar{n}}}\equiv 0
for
|i-i^{\prime}|\geq 2
.

Proof of Lemma
9.6
.

The proof of the claims for the intermediate cutoffs are nearly identical to that of
[
29
, Lemma 9.5]
, and we omit further details. The proof of the first claim for
\psi_{i,{q+{\bar{n}}}}
is nearly identical to that of
[
29
, Lemma 9.6]
, and so we omit further details. The proof of the second claim for
\psi_{i,{q+{\bar{n}}}}
is again essentially identical.
∎

Lemma 9.7

(
Lower order derivative bounds on
\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}
).

If
(x,t)\in\mathrm{supp\,}(\psi_{m,i_{m},j_{m},q+{\bar{n}}})
then

\displaystyle h_{m,j_{m},q+{\bar{n}}}\leq\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{(m+1)\left(i_{m}+1-i_{*}(j_{m})\right)}.

(9.9)

Moreover, if
i_{m}>i_{*}(j_{m})
we have
h_{m,j_{m},q+{\bar{n}}}\geq(\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}})\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{(m+1)(i_{m}-i_{*}(j_{m}))}
on the support of
\psi_{m,i_{m},j_{m},q+{\bar{n}}}
.
As a consequence, we have that for all
0\leq m,M\leq\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}
and
0\leq N\leq\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}
,

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q+{\bar{n}}-1}^{m}\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{m,i_{m},q+{\bar{n}}})}

\displaystyle\leq\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{i_{m}+1}(\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}})^{N}({\mu_{q+{\bar{n}}-1}^{-1}}\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{i_{m}+3})^{m}

(9.10a)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q+{\bar{n}}-1}^{M}\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,q+{\bar{n}}})}

\displaystyle\leq\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{i+1}(\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}})^{N}({\mu_{q+{\bar{n}}-1}^{-1}}\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{i+3})^{M}\,.

(9.10b)

Proof of Lemma
9.7
.

The proof of the first two estimates uses the definitions of cutoff functions in
[
29
, Lemma 5.5]
and
h_{m,j_{m},q+{\bar{n}}}
in (
9.4
). (
9.10a
) follows from (
9.6
) and (
9.9
). The proof of (
9.10b
) follows since we have employed the
maximum
over
m
of
i_{m}
to define
\psi_{i,q+{\bar{n}}}
in (
9.5
).
∎

Corollary 9.8

(
Higher order bounds on
\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}
).

For
N+M\leq 2\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}
and
i\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q+{\bar{n}}-1}^{M}\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,q+{\bar{n}}})}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{i+1}\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}(\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}})^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{i+3}{\mu_{q+{\bar{n}}-1}^{-1}},\mathrm{T}_{q+{\bar{n}}-1}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}-1}\right)\,.

(9.11)

Proof of Corollary
9.8
.

When
0\leq N\leq\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x},0\leq M\leq\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}\leq\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}}
, (
9.10b
) gives the result. For the remaining cases, either
N>\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}
or
M>\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}
, if
0\leq m\leq\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}
and
(x,t)\in\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{m,i_{m},q+{\bar{n}}}
, and so there exists
j_{m}\geq 0
with
i_{*}(j_{m})\leq i_{m}
such that
(x,t)\in\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{j_{m},q+{\bar{n}}-1}
. Thus we may appeal to Lemma
5.15
. Using that
i_{*}(j_{m})\leq i_{m}
implies
\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}-1}^{j_{m}}\leq\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{i_{m}}
, we deduce (
9.11
)
after using that either
N>\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}
or
M>\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}
, (
8.8b
), a large choice of
a
to absorb the implicit constant in the spare factor of
\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}}
, and taking the maximum over
m
from Definition
9.5
.
∎

Lemma 9.9

(
Spatial derivatives for the cutoffs).

Fix
q+{\bar{n}}\geq 1
,
0\leq m\leq\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}
, and
i_{m}\geq 0
. For all
j_{m}\geq 0
such that
i_{m}\geq i_{*}(j_{m})
, all
i\geq 0
, and all
N\leq\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}
, we have

\displaystyle{\bf 1}_{\mathrm{supp\,}(\psi_{j_{m},q+{\bar{n}}-1})}\frac{|D^{N}\psi_{m,i_{m},j_{m},q+{\bar{n}}}|}{\psi_{m,i_{m},j_{m},q+{\bar{n}}}^{1-N/\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}\lesssim(\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}})^{N}\,,\qquad\frac{|D^{N}\psi_{i,q+{\bar{n}}}|}{\psi_{i,q+{\bar{n}}}^{1-N/\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}\lesssim(\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}})^{N}\,.

(9.12)

Proof of Lemma
9.9
.

The proof of Lemma
9.9
is nearly identical to the proof of
[
29
, Lemma 9.9]
. The only parameter inequality which plays a role is (
8.8c
), which we note is the analogue of
[
29
, (11.14c)]
, which is used to absorb a lossy estimate for a high number of derivatives by the extra factors of
\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}}
present in (
9.12
); we refer to
[
29
, Lemma 9.9]
for further details.
∎

9.2.3 Maximal index appearing in the cutoff

Lemma 9.10

(
Maximal
i
index in the definition of
\psi_{i,q+{\bar{n}}}
).

There exists
{i_{\rm max}}={i_{\rm max}}(q+{\bar{n}})\geq 0
such that if
\lambda_{0}
is sufficiently large, then

\displaystyle\psi_{i,q+{\bar{n}}}

\displaystyle\equiv 0\quad\mbox{for all}\quad i>i_{\rm max}\,,\qquad\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{i_{\rm max}}

\displaystyle\leq\Gamma_{q}^{\nicefrac{{\mathsf{C_{\infty}}}}{{2}}+18}\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}r_{q}^{-1}\,,\qquad{i_{\rm max}}(q)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathsf{C_{\infty}}+12}{(b-1)\varepsilon_{\Gamma}}\,.

(9.13)

Proof of Lemma
9.10
.

The structure of this proof is identical to that of
[
29
, Lemma 9.10]
. Indeed the proof does not change as a result of the substitution of
\mu
for
\tau
. It only depends on an inductive assumption for
{i_{\rm max}}(q-1)
, and an inequality in which factors of
\mu
or
\tau
are balanced on both sides and therefore cancel; see
[
29
, (9.37)]
. We therefore omit further details.
∎

9.2.4 Mixed derivative estimates

We set
D_{q+{\bar{n}}}=\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}\cdot\nabla
, so that
D_{t,q+{\bar{n}}}=D_{t,q+{\bar{n}}-1}+D_{q+{\bar{n}}}
. Then we recall from
[
6
, equations (6.54)-(6.55)]
that

\displaystyle D_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{K}=\sum_{j=1}^{K}f_{j,K}D^{j}\,,\qquad\textnormal{ where }\quad f_{j,K}=\sum_{\{\gamma\in\mathbb{N}^{K}\colon|\gamma|=K-j\}}c_{j,K,\gamma}\prod_{\ell=1}^{K}D^{\gamma_{\ell}}\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}\,.

(9.14)

The
c_{j,K,\gamma}
’s are computable and depend only on
K,j
, and
\gamma
.

Lemma 9.11

(
Bounds for
D_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{K}
).

For
q+{\bar{n}}\geq 1
,
1\leq K\leq 2\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}
, any
a\leq 2\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}-K+j
, and any
0\leq i\leq{i_{\rm max}}(q+{\bar{n}})
, the functions
\{f_{j,K}\}_{j=1}^{K}
defined in (
9.14
) obey the estimate

\displaystyle\left\|D^{a}f_{j,K}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,q+{\bar{n}}})}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{i+1}\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}})^{K}(\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}})^{a+K-j}\,.

(9.15)

Proof of Lemma
9.11
.

The proof mirrors that of
[
29
, Lemma 9.11]
and follows from Corollary
9.8
with
M=0
and Lemma
5.15
; we omit further details.
∎

Lemma 9.12

(
Mixed derivatives for
\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}
).

For
q+{\bar{n}}\geq 1
and
0\leq i\leq{i_{\rm max}}
, we have that

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{K}D_{t,{q+{\bar{n}}}-1}^{M}\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,{q+{\bar{n}}}})}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\lesssim(\Gamma_{{q+{\bar{n}}}}^{i+1}\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}})^{K+1}(\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}})^{N+K}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{{q+{\bar{n}}}}^{i+3}{\mu_{{q+{\bar{n}}}-1}^{-1}},\mathrm{T}_{{q+{\bar{n}}}-1}^{-1}\Gamma_{{q+{\bar{n}}}-1}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\lesssim(\Gamma_{{q+{\bar{n}}}}^{i+1}\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}})(\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}})^{N}(\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{i-5}{{\mu_{{q+{\bar{n}}}}^{-1}}})^{K}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{i+3}{\mu_{{q+{\bar{n}}}-1}^{-1}},\mathrm{T}_{{q+{\bar{n}}}-1}^{-1}\Gamma_{{q+{\bar{n}}}-1}\right)

holds for
0\leq K+N+M\leq 2\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}
.

Proof of Lemma
9.12
.

The second estimate follows from (
8.5i
). In order to prove the first estimate, we use (
9.11
), (
9.14
), and (
9.15
). The proof mirrors that of
[
29
, Lemma 9.12]
, the first difference being that no factors of
r_{q}^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{3}}}
appear, and the second difference being that we save one factor of
\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{i+1}
out in front while converting all other such factors to
\mu_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{i-5}
instead of
\tau_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{i-5}
. We omit further details.
∎

Lemma 9.13

(
More mixed derivatives for
\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}
and derivatives for
\widehat{u}_{q+{\bar{n}}}
).

For
q+{\bar{n}}\geq 1
,
k\geq 1
,
\alpha,\beta\in{\mathbb{N}}^{k}
with
|\alpha|=K
,
|\beta|=M
, and
K+M\leq\nicefrac{{3\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{2}}+1
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\Big(\prod_{i=1}^{k}D^{\alpha_{i}}D_{t,{q+{\bar{n}}}-1}^{\beta_{i}}\Big)\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,{q+{\bar{n}}}})}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{i+1}\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}(\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}})^{K}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{i+3}{\mu_{{q+{\bar{n}}}-1}^{-1}},\Gamma_{{q+{\bar{n}}}-1}\mathrm{T}_{{q+{\bar{n}}}-1}^{-1}\right)\,.

(9.16)

Next, we have that

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}\Big(\prod_{i=1}^{k}D_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{\alpha_{i}}D_{t,{q+{\bar{n}}}-1}^{\beta_{i}}\Big)\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,{q+{\bar{n}}}})}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim(\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{i+1}\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}})^{K+1}(\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}})^{N+K}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{i+3}{\mu_{{q+{\bar{n}}}-1}^{-1}},\Gamma_{{q+{\bar{n}}}-1}\mathrm{T}_{{q+{\bar{n}}}-1}^{-1}\right)

(9.17a)

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{i+1}\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}(\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}})^{N}(\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{i-5}{\mu_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{-1}})^{K}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{i+3}{\mu_{{q+{\bar{n}}}-1}^{-1}},\Gamma_{{q+{\bar{n}}}-1}\mathrm{T}_{{q+{\bar{n}}}-1}^{-1}\right)

(9.17b)

holds for all
0\leq K+M+N\leq\nicefrac{{3\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{2}}+1
. Lastly, we have the estimate

\displaystyle\left\|\Big(\prod_{i=1}^{k}D^{\alpha_{i}}D_{t,{q+{\bar{n}}}}^{\beta_{i}}\Big)D\widehat{u}_{q+{\bar{n}}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,{q+{\bar{n}}}})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{i+2}\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}(\lambda_{{q+{\bar{n}}}}\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}})^{K}

\displaystyle\qquad\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{i-5}{\mu_{{q+{\bar{n}}}}^{-1}},\Gamma_{{q+{\bar{n}}}-1}\mathrm{T}_{{q+{\bar{n}}}-1}^{-1}\right)

(9.18)

for all
K+M\leq\nicefrac{{3\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{2}}
, the estimate

\displaystyle\left\|\Big(\prod_{i=1}^{k}D^{\alpha_{i}}D_{t,{q+{\bar{n}}}}^{\beta_{i}}\Big)\widehat{u}_{q+{\bar{n}}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,{q+{\bar{n}}}})}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{i+2}\delta_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{2}(\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}})^{K}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{i-5}{\mu_{{q+{\bar{n}}}}^{-1}},\Gamma_{{q+{\bar{n}}}-1}\mathrm{T}_{{q+{\bar{n}}}-1}^{-1}\right)

(9.19)

for all
K+M\leq\nicefrac{{3\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{2}}+1
, and the estimate

\displaystyle\left\|D^{K}\partial_{t}^{M}\widehat{u}_{q+{\bar{n}}}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}(\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}})^{K}\mathrm{T}_{{q+{\bar{n}}}}^{-M}

(9.20)

for all
K+M\leq 2\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}
.

Proof of Lemma
9.13
.

We note that (
9.17b
) follows directly from (
9.17a
) by appealing to (
8.5i
). The proof then proceeds by proving (
9.16
), then (
9.17a
), and finally (
9.18
)–(
9.20
).

Proof of (
9.16
).

The statement is proven by induction on
k
and mirrors the proof of
[
29
, (9.42)]
, the first difference being that no factors of
r_{q}^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{3}}}
appear, and the second difference being the substitution of
\mu
for
\tau
. We omit further details.

Proof of (
9.17a
).

To prove this estimate, we use
[
29
, Lemma A.6]
; since the proof is nearly identical to
[
29
, (9.43a)]
, we omit further details.

Proofs of (
9.18
)–(
9.19
).

The proofs of these estimates use the parameter inequality (
8.5i
),
[
29
, Lemma A.6]
, and induction on
k
. Since the method is identical to
[
29
, (9.44)–(9.45)]
, we omit further details.

Proof of (
9.20
).
The proof of this bound is immediate from Lemma
5.15
, the definition of
\widehat{w}_{q+{\bar{n}}}
in Definition
5.10
, the inductive assumption (
2.25b
), and the triangle inequality.
∎

9.2.5 Material derivatives

Lemma 9.14

(
Mixed spatial and material derivatives for velocity cutoffs).

Let
q+{\bar{n}}\geq 1
,
0\leq i\leq i_{\rm max}(q+{\bar{n}})
,
N,K,M,k\geq 0
, and let
\alpha,\beta\in{\mathbb{N}}^{k}
be such that
|\alpha|=K
and
|\beta|=M
. Then

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\psi_{i,q+{\bar{n}}}^{1-(K+M)/\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}\left|\left(\prod_{l=1}^{k}D^{\alpha_{l}}D_{t,q+{\bar{n}}-1}^{\beta_{l}}\right)\psi_{i,q+{\bar{n}}}\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim(\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}})^{K}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t},\Gamma_{{q+{\bar{n}}}}^{i+3}{\mu_{q+{\bar{n}}-1}^{-1}},\Gamma_{{q+{\bar{n}}}+1}\mathrm{T}_{{q+{\bar{n}}}-1}^{-1}\right)

(9.21)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\psi_{i,{q+{\bar{n}}}}^{1-(N+K+M)/\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}\left|D^{N}\left(\prod_{l=1}^{k}D_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{\alpha_{l}}D_{t,{q+{\bar{n}}}-1}^{\beta_{l}}\right)\psi_{i,{q+{\bar{n}}}}\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim(\lambda_{q+{\bar{n}}}\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}})^{N}(\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{i-5}\tau_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{-1})^{K}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t},\Gamma_{{q+{\bar{n}}}}^{i+3}{\mu_{q+{\bar{n}}-1}^{-1}},\Gamma_{{q+{\bar{n}}}+1}\mathrm{T}_{{q+{\bar{n}}}-1}^{-1}\right)

(9.22)

where the first bound holds for
K+M\leq\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}
, and the second for
N+K+M\leq\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}
.

Proof of Lemma
9.14
.

The method of proof is identical to
[
29
, Lemma 9.16]
and only requires substituting squared cutoff functions for cutoff functions raised to the sixth power, and replacing
\tau
with
\mu
; we omit further details.
∎

9.2.6
L^{r}
size of the velocity cutoffs

We now show that (
2.15
) holds with
q^{\prime}={q+{\bar{n}}}
.

Lemma 9.15

(
Support estimate).

For all
0\leq i\leq{i_{\rm max}}({q+{\bar{n}}})
and
1\leq r\leq\infty
, we have that
\|\psi_{i,{q+{\bar{n}}}}\|_{r}\lesssim\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{\frac{-2i+{\mathsf{C}_{b}}}{r}}
where
{\mathsf{C}_{b}}
is defined in (
2.15
) and thus depends only on
b
.

Proof of Lemma
9.15
.

The proof follows exactly the method of
[
29
, Lemma 9.17]
. The main difference is that squares are substituted for cubes, and the definition of
{\mathsf{C}_{b}}
from (
2.15
) has been adjusted accordingly. We therefore omit further details and refer the reader to
[
29
, Lemma 9.17]
.
∎

9.2.7 Verifying Eqn. (
2.16
)

Lemma 9.16

(
Overlapping and timescales).

Let
q^{\prime\prime}\in\{q+1,\dots,q+{\bar{n}}-1\}
. Assume that
\psi_{i,q+{\bar{n}}}\psi_{i^{\prime\prime},q^{\prime\prime}}\not\equiv 0
. Then it must be the case that
\mu_{q+{\bar{n}}}\Gamma_{q+{\bar{n}}}^{-i}\leq\mu_{q^{\prime\prime}}\Gamma_{q^{\prime\prime}}^{-i^{\prime\prime}-{25}}
.

Proof of Lemma
9.16
.

The proof of this Lemma is identical to
[
29
, Lemma 9.18]
, save for substituting
\mu
for
\tau
, and so we omit further details.
∎
